# Unravis backlog and checklist

One shared list for every chat and session. Open it before starting work and tick items when they are done and verified.

## How to use this file

- **Add an item:** append one line under the right section. Format: `- [ ] **BL-NNN** Title. One line of why or what. (source: chat/date)`. Take the next free number.
- **Tick an item:** change `[ ]` to `[x]` only when it works on the real data path (project rule in `CLAUDE.md`) and add `(done YYYY-MM-DD, rcNN)` at the end. Say plainly if it is verified on live data or not: `live-verified` or `demo-only-verified`.
- **Never delete an item.** If it is dropped, change it to `[~]` and write why.
- **Deferred** means the owner said "not now", with the reason. Do not build a deferred item without the owner's go.
- Keep each line short. Detail goes in the plan under `.forge/<work-item>/`.

Legend: `[ ]` open, `[x]` done, `[~]` dropped.

A refusal stops the start with the fixed "not locked" sentence and logs `sql: settings-not-locked (<word>, <stage>)`, except for an OPEN-stage `access` or `unreadable` (the open of the file was refused; the reader tags every refusal with a fixed stage `pre-open`, `open` or `after-open`, and the same two words after the file was opened stop the start, sweep finding 2, 2026-11-22; the log line ends `(<word>, <stage>)`): for that one case only (and only when the reader itself tagged it), a TEMPORARY fallback logs one line and uses the old by-path proof plus read. `sharing`, unknown words and untagged errors stop. The fallback is weaker (no link-count or final-path check, no share-deny-write, unbounded read) and re-opens the proof-to-read window; a person who controls a folder in the path can force `access` and so reach it (design doc decision 7). Fake-reader tests pass on Linux; `GOOS=windows go vet` is clean; the Windows tests have not run; the real reader has not run as `NT SERVICE\UnravisBlackboxSql`. P1 is still owed; do not tick BL-140 or BL-141 until it passes. The `open` stage is not live-proven: until probe P1 runs on a PC it is held only by pure tests, a source scan of the package and Windows CI tests that have not run.

- [ ] **BL-551** Blackbox SQL "Everything" profile, extras round 1 (grants, workers, pendingio, latches, rg; `internal/bbsql/extras_*.go`): written and unit-tested, NOT live-verified. Columns come from Microsoft Learn, no real SQL Server has run them. Tick only after a run on a real SQL Server (2016-2022) with `sql.profile` = everything shows the five kinds in `bb-sql-*.ndjson` with sane numbers, and a login without VIEW SERVER STATE switches the extra off without stopping the Standard samplers. Numbers only: no names, paths or query text.
  - Known limit: `bb:latches` keeps the top 20 classes by cumulative wait time, so a brand-new spike in a low-ranked class can stay hidden until it passes the top 20. A stats reset is seen only when a counter goes backwards (same as waits).

- [ ] **BL-575** Blackbox SQL deep profile: `bb:qhash` (top 50 query hashes by cumulative CPU, per-hash deltas of execs, CPU, elapsed, reads every 5 minutes) and `bb:qtext` (scrubbed statement text for the top 40 hashes, only with profile deep AND `sql.queryText` on; every text through `querytext.Scrub`, a hash re-sent only after 6 hours). Written and unit-tested in `internal/bbsql/extras_qhash.go` and `extras_qtext.go`, NOT live-verified. The host sets `Runner.QueryTextMaxChars` from `Config.SQLQueryText()` (`sqlQueryTextChars` in `cmd/unravis-blackbox/sqlrunner.go`); statement text goes only to `bb-sqlq-<hour>.ndjson`, which `/sql/files/` and the keeper do not serve, and the same age and byte sweep applies. Tick only after a run on a real SQL Server (2016-2022) with VIEW SERVER STATE: qhash lines have sane deltas, qtext lines appear only with the flag on, hold `?` for every literal and no credential, and the plan-cache CROSS APPLY cost on a busy server is acceptable. Known limits: both rank by cumulative CPU, not CPU in the interval, so a hash that just became hot needs time to appear; the text APPLY runs over every cached plan before the TOP; a statement holding a credential word is withheld whole; encrypted procedures return NULL text and are skipped.

(source: owner chat 2026-11-16)
- [ ] **BL-136** Update the installed Blackbox recorder from the app. Today the program under `C:Program FilesUnravis Blackbox` cannot be replaced: Install refuses with "exists" and Apply only saves settings. New op `update` (one administrator approval, same guard as install): stops the service if it ran, writes the bundled `unravis-blackbox.exe` beside the old one, checks its SHA-256, renames it into place (old copy kept as `unravis-blackbox.exe.old`), restarts only if it ran, then waits for running and a status answer (no read key needed); on any failure puts the old program back and restarts it. Config, key and recordings are never touched and the folder lock is not loosened. "Already up to date" when the hashes match. The Blackbox status panel shows an "Update the recorder" row when the installed recorder reports an older version than the bundled one (`POST /api/blackbox/this-pc/update`; list fields `recorderVersion`, `programVersion`, `updateAvailable`). **Built with unit, server and web tests; NOT run for real:** needs a live elevated run on a real PC (the replace over a locked Program Files exe, the real `unravis-blackbox version` check, the status probe against the real service and the rollback on a forced failure). The SQL service (BL-101/102/118/119) wrote `bb-sql-<hour>.ndjson` and nothing read it. **Built 2026-11-23 (branch `claude/project-thread-6999ym`), unit-tested, NOT checked on a live SQL Server:** the recorder serves the files on `/sql/files/<name>` behind the read key (its own account may read the locked `\sql` folder, the person's account may not); the keeper allows that one path; `ReadSQLSamples` re-checks every line (known kind, fixed fields, row caps, wait-type shape); the series route adds `sqlDmv.*` lines (requests and blockers, wait-stat deltas for the 40 busiest wait types, schedulers, memory-grant semaphores, tempdb, file-stall time per read and write, availability-group state) and a `sql` block saying why a line is missing (locked folder, damaged lines, row-limit cuts). Charts group "SQL Server: deeper checks". Owner decision to confirm: the recorder now serves the SQL numbers to anyone holding the read key (before, nobody but administrators could read them); the files hold counts, milliseconds, ids and wait types only. Live check owed: BL-141 (install-sql, grant, enable-sql), then open Charts on that Blackbox and compare Waits and Requests against `sys.dm_os_wait_stats` in SSMS.
- [ ] **BL-547** Blackbox SQL: check access before asking for anything (owner chat 2026-11-23). "Check Blackbox access" on the SQL connection form tests, inside the key keeper (the saved password never reaches the app): connect, the rights ceiling (C8), a read test of each sampler's fixed statement and a write warning for databases the login can change. A grant script is shown only when a right is missing, or when the login has too many rights. New settings `sql.profile` (standard, everything, deep) and `sql.extras` in `blackbox.json` (no server, login or password can be put there). **Built 2026-11-23, unit-tested, NOT checked on a live SQL Server** (BL-141 live check owed): connect, read, rights and write verdicts, and the script text, need the owner's server.
- [ ] **BL-548** Blackbox SQL: a stored SQL login on the recorder host, and a server on another machine (owner chat 2026-11-23: other PCs, "if you block it it is of no use"). **Built 2026-11-23, unit-tested on Linux, NOT checked on Windows or a live SQL Server:** `set-sql-login` (administrator; stdin or a hash-bound file the helper only reads and Unravis removes) and `clear-sql-login`; the login is sealed with DPAPI (this PC) in `<folder>\sql\login.sealed`, never in `blackbox.json`, packs, logs, status or errors; the SQL service reads it at start and a file it cannot read stops the service (no silent fall back); `remove-sql` deletes it; a remote server needs a login, TLS stays on and the C8 ceiling still runs; `trustServerCertificate` is an explicit owner choice. Owed: live check on a Windows host (DPAPI seal, folder access of the service, a real remote server). The setup step in the app that calls these commands is the next item (BL-549).
- [ ] **BL-549** Blackbox SQL: turn collection on from the app (owner chat 2026-11-23). **Built 2026-11-23, unit-tested on Linux, UI audit screen x-blackbox-sql-collect passes, NOT checked on Windows or a live SQL Server:** `setup-sql` (one elevated call: install the SQL service if needed, save the login sealed, start; Windows sign-in prints the grant and leaves the service latched off until the owner enables it), keeper op `sql.loginfile` (the saved password never reaches the app process), routes `POST /api/blackbox/this-pc/sql/setup` and `/sql/login/clear`, and the "Collect SQL data" panel on the Blackbox status screen (access check first, profile standard/everything/deep, remove saved login). Owed: live check on Windows (UAC prompt, service start, a remote server). Still to build: query hash level, opt-in query text (scrubbed), a status line for the SQL service and login, a keeper call that checks access and writes the file in one step.
- [ ] **BL-550** Blackbox SQL: query text (opt-in) and query hash levels (owner chat 2026-11-23). **App side built 2026-11-23, unit-tested on Linux, NOT checked on Windows or a live SQL Server:** `internal/querytext` (`Scrub`: withholds any statement that may carry a credential, drops comments, turns every literal into ?, runs secretscrub as a second net, caps size, fuzzed), config `sql.queryText` and `sql.queryTextMaxChars` (kept only for profile deep; `Config.SQLQueryText()`), the switch with its warning and size on the Collect SQL data panel, and the SQL service status line. The sampler thread owns the statements (`bb:qhash`, `bb:qtext`, registered only when the flag is on) and must pass every kept text through `querytext.Scrub`. Owed: those statements, a live check that real query text is scrubbed, a pack/case check that text stays out unless chosen.
- [ ] **BL-555** Blackbox SQL "Everything" profile, extras batch B (`internal/bbsql/extras_*.go`: perfctr, mem, memclerks, conns, trans, waiting, agdetail, locks, facts, factsdb, factscfg): written and unit-tested on Linux, NOT live-verified. Columns come from Microsoft Learn and the counter types from the sys.dm_os_performance_counters page; no real SQL Server has run these statements. Tick only after a run on a real SQL Server (2016-2022) with `sql.profile` = everything shows all eleven kinds in `bb-sql-*.ndjson` with sane numbers, a login without VIEW SERVER STATE switches only the extra off, and perfctr shows the expected short keys (a counter whose type differs from the table is dropped, so a missing key is the sign). Numbers only: no names, paths or query text (perfctr turns instance names into database ids with DB_ID, the facts version is digits and dots only). Known limits: `waiting` gives blocked count and distinct blockers, not a chain depth (a depth needs a recursive walk); `conns` reads sessions only (no dm_exec_connections); `facts` settings and database options carry numeric ids and the reader maps ids to names; per-database perfctr rows are cut at 1024 rows, which is reached at about 90 databases (the server-wide rows sort first and are always kept; the highest database ids are the ones cut; marked truncated); `trans` is light (one COUNT over a small DMV); `locks` reads one row per lock, so it runs only every 60 s with the 5 s query limit and the back-off as its bound; `agdetail` rates are the lowest reported, and 0 is an idle or caught-up replica Part of BL-552 (round 2).
- [ ] **BL-148** One source wizard: create-a-case and add-to-a-case use the same Add sources screen (Find on this PC, Mongo, SQL Server, Redis, Files, Blackbox, Another day; rail, tray, Open folder, Blackbox folder detection). Spec and prototype in `docs/source-wizard/`. **Checked live in the app (isolated config):** Find on this PC lists real items; Blackbox finds the real recorder folder, Check it works says Works, the recorder is added; create-case with the recorder works; add-to-case adds a ticked Windows event log and shows "Already in this case". **Not checked live:** Mongo/SQL forms with the address filled in from a scan (no Mongo or SQL on this PC to scan; build now prefills the usual local address and flips the row to Added, covered by tests only), closed-case message, Redis add by address. Round 2 (checked live): file drop stages the file, Review text, Back focus. **Leftovers (minor):** a hub tab switch while adding drops an unfinished list and any failure shown; a reset during a run is not guarded; a case that "changed somewhere else" is not detected before the draft question; a Blackbox recorder is saved before the run and nothing undoes it. The first three leftovers are built as BL-520 (demo API only). Stays unticked until the owner has tried it. (source: owner chat 2026-11-19)

(source: PR #2 review SEC-10, 2026-11-15) **Built 2026-11-19 (unit tests; next PR after #15):** `addressOnly` now cuts a login whose password holds a "/" and a "?" or "#" before the "@". A digits-only password like that reads exactly like `host:port/path`, so parsing alone cannot tell them apart. `noteOpenedSource` therefore cuts through the typed password first (`addressWithout`, plain or URL-escaped). Still open: a digits-only password typed only inside the address, with no password field, is not caught. **Review fix 2026-11-19 (PR #19):** a password with `?` or `#` and no `/` is cut too (`user:pw?x@host/db`); when the first `@` sits inside the password, the host is the text after the last `@` before the path. Limit: a login followed by a query with an `@` and no path is read as part of the password.
- [ ] **BL-037** DATA-10: a call that starts in one minute and ends in a later one is counted whole in the first minute; and the 24-hour look-back can be cut short by the 50,000-row cap. Spread the time across minutes and say when the cap is hit. (source: PR #2 review DATA-10, 2026-11-15) **Built 2026-11-19 (branch `claude/project-thread-g6h1y3`), unit-tested only, not checked live.** The imported Query Store file lane now spreads each interval over the minutes it runs in the window (the part before or after the window is left out). The look-back read before the window is tried at 24 h, then 1 h, then 15 min when a read hits the 50,000-row cap (the store lists oldest first, so a capped read drops the rows nearest the window); the lane says "hit the 50,000-row limit" only when an interval may still be missing. Not done: the saved live Query Store day (held-sql lane) still keeps each block whole at its start and leaves the web to spread it; same fix could apply there. Live check: import a Query Store export with 15- or 60-minute intervals and open the Timeline on a 1-hour window.
- [ ] **BL-038** DATA-04 and DATA-11: add `SumMs` to `LaneSeriesPoint` so the lane no longer depends on `round1` rounding of the average. (source: PR #2 review DATA-04/DATA-11, 2026-11-15) **Checked 2026-11-19: already covered, suggest closing.** `seriesOf` saves the average as the exact quotient `sum_ms / execs` (no `round1` when a sum is read), and `mergeGroups` recomputes it exactly, so average x executions gives the summed time back (tests in `lane_sql_sum_test.go`). Adding a `SumMs` field was tried and dropped: a full 3000-point series then comes close to the 256 KB saved-report limit (about 214 KB today, about 245 KB with the field), so a long read could fail to save. Only older saved days (before the DATA-11 fix) still hold a rounded average.
- [ ] **BL-039** DATA-09: tell a Query Store state lane apart from an imported-file lane in the reason text; check the fallbacks that run after a Query Store state lane; handle a stale state from the newest run. (source: PR #2 review DATA-09, 2026-11-15) **Built 2026-11-19 (branch `claude/project-thread-g6h1y3`), unit-tested only, not checked live.** Query Store state reasons now start "From the saved database check (not an imported file):"; a state saved before the window ended adds when it was saved and that it may have changed; the state is taken only from the source whose saved days drew the lane, not a newer day of another server. Fallbacks after a state lane checked: an imported Query Store file, the Blackbox and api-seen may still replace it (better evidence); the capped Query Store file note is now added to the state reason instead of replacing it while the qs-off code stayed. Live check: a case whose saved SQL day has Query Store off or read-only.

- [ ] **BL-149** Auto-updater for Unravis and the Blackbox: signed manifest (ed25519), opt-in background check, verified download, hand-off to `unravis-setup --upgrade`, rollback, offline import. Slices U1 to U7 in the spec. Not started: All decisions answered (D1 to D14) as of 2026-11-20; D14 (pick a licence) is nachi's. Includes Blackbox self-update (U6) and Unravis Lite (U7).
- [ ] **BL-150** Auto-updater U1 (signing tools) built 2026-11-20: `internal/update` and `tools/release/sigtool` pass unit tests and two reviews; `Sign-Release.ps1` and the release.yml manifest step are NOT run (no PowerShell here, no real draft). Stays unticked until: keys made (docs/auto-updater/KEYS.md), an rc tag builds a draft with `update-manifest.json`, `Sign-Release.ps1` signs it and `-Feed` publishes it. U2 (feed fetcher in `internal/update/fetch.go`, unit-tested against a fake HTTPS feed, two reviews, called by the app from U3, still only run against a fake feed). U3 (2026-11-20, owner said go): background check every 30 min (changeable, can be off) in `internal/server/updater.go`, `GET /api/update/status`, `PUT /api/update/settings`, Updates section in the Help panel, update bar with Not now; the old GitHub-API check is gone. Two reviews of U3. NOT verified live: there is no signed feed and no trusted key yet, so a real build shows "unreachable" until keys are made and a signed rc is published; UI checked in a browser against the demo build only. U4 (2026-11-20): download of the setup file into the per-user cache (`%LOCALAPPDATA%\\Unravis\\updates`), size and SHA-256 checked against the signed manifest, hash re-checked with the file held open right before it starts, `unravis-setup --upgrade --expect-sha256 --expect-version` (window, silent and elevated worker refuse a different file or version), Update now / Install buttons in the update bar. NOT verified on Windows or with a real feed: the file-holding code, the installer hand-off and the UAC step are Windows-only and were only compiled (GOOS=windows vet) and unit-tested with fakes. U7 (2026-11-20): Unravis Lite, `cmd/unravis-lite` + `internal/lite` + `web/lite`: a console viewer for one Blackbox folder or share (default: the Blackbox on this PC, or `--folder`), loopback, one-time link, route allowlist in front of the real Blackbox server code, one-entry in-memory list, no key, no writes. Tested with the real recorder's files through the real series code (Linux, with a path override) and in a browser; release.yml packs `unravis-lite-vX-windows-x64.zip` (manifest `lite` asset). Windows check on nachi's PC (2026-11-20, normal user): Lite on a scratch recording copy (list, charts, one-time link, 401 without token, --open, Ctrl+C), the zip step (Windows PowerShell 5.1), U4 LaunchSetup hash/hold/early-exit, and `go test ./...` (76 packages ok) all passed. NOT verified: admin run with the real installed Blackbox, a UNC share, the manifest step with all assets, pwsh 7. Lite self-update is not built yet. U5 (2026-11-20): rollback. Installer step `keep-previous` keeps the old setup + marker in `.previous`; the app counts failed starts (`internal/update/health.go`, `cmd/audit-explorer/health.go`), skips the bad version and opens the old setup after 3 failed starts. Unit-tested (tracker, hand-over, crash vs clean exit, skip list in the updater, mutation checks); the installer step tests are written but only run on Windows. NOT verified on a real install: the whole rollback path (forced bad update on a scratch PC), no Help-panel message yet. U6 (2026-11-20): Blackbox service self-update (`internal/blackbox/selfupdate.go`): modes install/notify/off in `blackbox.json` `autoUpdate` (default: not written, so older recorder programs still read the file); checks the signed feed every 30 min or more, downloads the Blackbox zip, checks the exe hash against the signed manifest, starts `unravis-blackbox update` (existing swap with `.old` and rollback); a pending marker decides success next start, a failed version is skipped. A Blackbox installed by Unravis carries `managed-by-unravis` and does not self-update (Unravis pins its hash). Unit-tested on Linux only. NOT verified on Windows or against a real feed or service. Folder/share source and maintenance window not built. U8 (2026-11-20): `unravis-setup.exe --verify <folder>` (`internal/update/offline.go`, `cmd/unravis-setup/verify.go`) checks `update-manifest.json` + `.sig` + listed files offline with the same signature, channel and hash checks, prints OK/not here, installs nothing; docs `docs/auto-updater/OFFLINE.md`. Unit-tested; NOT verified on a real signed release (no keys yet) and the in-app Import button is not built. U1b (2026-11-20): release.yml attests the release files; Sign-Release.ps1 verifies the attestations (workflow + commit + tag ref) before signing. NOT run on a real rc: the `gh attestation verify --format json` field names (`sourceRepositoryDigest`, `sourceRepositoryRef`) and the attest step are unproven until an rc tag is built and Sign-Release.ps1 is run. Stays unticked until then. (source: owner chat 2026-11-20) (source: owner chat 2026-11-20)

Live-verified on rc.59's own logs (2026-11-22), which showed four bugs, fixed in the same pass: (1) the saved CI log hid everything after line ~1270 as one unclosed private key, so Go tests were blank (key blocks are now bounded and end on a non-key line; Go jobs show as "Go tests", 7,393 passed / 0 failed / 3 skipped); (2) a build that skips tests because CI already passed said "Nothing found", it now says "Skipped on purpose" and names the CI run; (3) the run log showed garbled marks (a tick as "Γ£ô"), fixed at write time (UTF-8 console in `Release.ps1`) and repaired when read for old logs; (4) the Build step log was about 932 lines of repeated "Refreshing run status", now folded to the latest copy (351 lines for the whole run). Not yet seen on a live release: the write-time UTF-8 fix (3), which needs the next rc. Related BL-198, BL-199, BL-501 (Releases app auto-update, was BL-199), BL-502 (was BL-461). (2026-11-22)

At start the app runs it from its program folder when that setup knows the flag (checked by the help text), at most 3 starts per journal; an older setup gets a note ("run Setup > Repair"). ## Blackbox expansion (design and reviews done; groups 1-8 built on branch `bbx/all-groups` (PR #12), awaiting live checks)

Owner ruling 2026-11-13: nothing here is "deferred": an item is either in the backlog or dropped. This replaces the 2026-11-12 "wait for the owner's go" ruling for the Blackbox. The owner said not to implement yet: first finalize the backlog and conditions. Conditions every item must meet, the order, and the SQL security review are in `docs/research/blackbox-expansion-prioritised.md` and `docs/research/blackbox-sql-security-review.md`. Items marked [Must] come first. Items marked "Built, awaiting live check" have code and tests on `bbx/all-groups` but are NOT verified on a real server, so they stay unticked until the owner's live checks pass.

- [ ] **BL-100** Blackbox SQL performance-counter group (Batch Requests/sec, User Connections, Lock Waits/sec, Processes Blocked). No SQL login needed, only host counter access. Needs a change to the Blackbox recorder, which today reads only its own machine. (source: Query Store chat, 2026-11-12) **Built, awaiting live check (PR #12).**
- [ ] **BL-101** Blackbox sampler of `dm_exec_requests` and sessions every 15 s: running at once by wait type, blocking. Needs `VIEW SERVER PERFORMANCE STATE` (`VIEW SERVER STATE` before SQL 2022), no stored password. Opus security review done 2026-11-13: allowed with conditions C1-C14 (BL-123, BL-124); output only wait type, counts, max wait ms, blocked and head-blocker counts. (source: same) **Built, awaiting live check (PR #12).**
- [ ] **BL-102** Blackbox sampler of wait-stats deltas every 60 s, top 20, benign waits filtered, no query text. Query-stats deltas dropped by the security review (scans the whole plan cache, unbounded state; Query Store already holds that history). Same conditions as BL-101. (source: same) **Built, awaiting live check (PR #12).**
- [ ] **BL-103** Show Blackbox SQL data as a lane source with its own label ("Blackbox sampling every 15 s since <date>") once BL-100 or BL-101 exist. (source: same) **Built (BBX-5-16, `internal/server/tracing_blackbox.go`, 17 tests pass), awaiting a live check with a real Blackbox that has SQL counters.** Found already built 2026-11-21; the backlog line was stale.

### Blackbox expansion (research 2026-11-13; MoSCoW, order and open questions in `docs/research/blackbox-expansion-prioritised.md`)
Scope rule from the owner: per-process detail only for the app pools and services the owner selects (payroll APIs, email service). Servers run a mix of .NET Framework 4.7.2 and .NET 10 (phased migration), so the runtime is detected per process automatically. The Blackbox must stay light (BL-122) and never pause, restart or dump a process.
- [ ] **BL-104** [Must] HTTP.sys kernel queue sampler per app pool. Sample `\HTTP Service Request Queues(*)\CurrentQueueSize`, `MaxQueueItemAge`, `RejectedRequests`, `ArrivalRate` every 15 s to prove queueing before w3wp.exe. (source: research, 2026-11-13) **Built, awaiting live check (PR #12).**
- [ ] **BL-105** [Must] Command-line-free PID resolver for IIS app pools and Windows services. Map w3wp.exe PIDs via `W3SVC_W3WP` (`<PID>_<AppPoolName>`) and services via `QueryServiceStatusEx`; key on (PID, creation time) so recycles, PID reuse and `w3wp#N` shifts are safe. Also detect each process's runtime on its own: `clr.dll` loaded = .NET Framework, `coreclr.dll`/`hostfxr.dll` = .NET 10; re-check only when the process set changes. A .NET 10 pool may be in-process (inside w3wp.exe) or out-of-process (a child dotnet/app exe): handle both. (source: research, 2026-11-13) **Built, awaiting live check (PR #12).**
- [ ] **BL-106** [Must] IIS worker-process active requests, threads and WAS ping latency for selected app pools every 15 s. (source: research, 2026-11-13) **Built, awaiting live check (PR #12).**
- [ ] **BL-107** [Must] Extend BL-100 with no-login SQL counters: Memory Grants Pending, Page life expectancy, Free list stalls/sec, SQL (Re-)Compilations/sec, Deadlocks/sec, Lock Wait Time, Log Flush Wait Time, Forwarded Records/sec; default and named instances (`MSSQL$<name>:`). (source: research, 2026-11-13) **Built, awaiting live check (PR #12).**
- [ ] **BL-108** [Must] Disk read/write latency split, IOPS, split IO, processor queue length and privileged/DPC/interrupt CPU time every 15 s. (source: research, 2026-11-13) **Built, awaiting live check (PR #12).**
- [ ] **BL-109** [Should, used for .NET Framework 4.7.2 targets] Per-process CLR counters for selected targets: GC heap and generation sizes, % time in GC, allocation rate, contention, threads, exceptions/sec. (source: research, 2026-11-13) **Built, awaiting live check (PR #12).**
- [ ] **BL-110** [Should, used for .NET 10 targets] Per-process EventPipe collector for selected targets (System.Runtime and AspNetCore.Hosting counters) with no restart. First prove on a real app pool that SYSTEM can open `\\.\pipe\dotnet-diagnostic-{pid}` (not confirmed) and measure the overhead inside the target; if either fails, fall back to Process counters plus `GetProcessMemoryInfo` for .NET 10. (source: research, 2026-11-13) **Built, awaiting live check (PR #12).**
  - Groomed 2026-11-21 (`docs/pipeline/BL-110-126-113-groom.md`): this line says built, but PR #12 only built the `proc10` PDH fallback; there is no EventPipe client. Gated steps: (1) read-only `diag-probe` (the SYSTEM-can-open-the-pipe proof), (2) codec, (3) NetTrace reader, (4) `dotnetrt` group, off until overhead is measured. New Blackbox group, no new service; condition 2 ("no attach") conflicts, owner to confirm.
  - Review follow-ups (2026-11-22, #99 and #116, none ticked): an administrator who is not SYSTEM may be denied the token read on an IIS app-pool process (fails safe, not tried on a real app pool); `DefaultDialer()` guards `$TMPDIR` by comment only (fixed 2026-11-22: `DefaultDialer()` never reads it, only `CLIDialer()` for the command does); CI lint runs on Linux only, so the Windows-only `nolint` lines are not CI-checked. Live proof still owed: `diag-probe` on a real .NET server as SYSTEM (BL-110 stays unticked).
  - Next steps (2026-11-22): the remaining plan steps are all gated or optional. S2a reader, S2b counters and S3 `dotnetrt` need the owner answer on BL-126 "no attach" (and S3 a new collector group, deferred); D8 (CI proof on the Windows runner) is optional and edits `ci.yml`. So this pass built only the review follow-ups: `CLIDialer()` vs `DefaultDialer()` (structural TMPDIR guard), a usage note that IIS app-pool targets need SYSTEM, and a comment fix. Built and unit-tested, not live-verified; `diag-probe` on a real .NET server as SYSTEM is still owed. Not ticked.
- [ ] **BL-111** [Should] Windows event log tailer without message text: disk, commit and port exhaustion, WAS recycle and application crash event IDs. (source: research, 2026-11-13) **Built, awaiting live check (PR #12).**
- [ ] **BL-112** [Should] TCP retransmit, reset, outbound queue and TCP state counts (no remote addresses) every 15 s. (source: research, 2026-11-13) **Built, awaiting live check (PR #12).**
- [ ] **BL-113** [Could, VMs only] Hyper-V and VMware CPU steal and memory ballooning counters. (source: research, 2026-11-13)
  - Groomed 2026-11-21: build VMware balloon (`vm` group) now; hold CPU steal until a real VMware guest capture exists; drop Hyper-V and clouds. The group needs a "not applicable" outcome on physical servers. Cannot be live-proven without a VMware guest.
  - Built (PRs #86, #88 and this follow-up): group `vm`, VMware balloon only (`vm.balloonBytes`). `unravis-blackbox vm-probe` is in PR #90. A host without VMware Tools writes nothing and leaves nothing in Missing (proven on a real Windows PDH, the GitHub hosted runner, without VMware). NOT proven: the counter name or unit, any nonzero value, any real VMware guest. CPU steal is not built: it needs `vm-probe` output from a real VMware guest. Hyper-V, Azure, AWS and GCP have no guest-visible counter (spec 2026-11-21). Stays unticked until a real VMware guest is read.
  - Review follow-ups (2026-11-22): #114 merged (quiet not-applicable recheck, re-detect a vanished counter); #119 covers the two review SHOULDs (a `vm` group built after startup, then Tools removed, still showed `vm: slow`; a failing counter reopened its query every minute). Nothing is verified on a real VMware guest: run `unravis-blackbox vm-probe` on one and send the output.

- [ ] **BL-115** [Should] Hard page faults, kernel pool bytes and pagefile usage. (source: research, 2026-11-13) **Built, awaiting live check (PR #12).**
- [ ] **BL-116** [Should] Per-process I/O, pagefile bytes, virtual bytes and elapsed time for selected targets. (source: research, 2026-11-13) **Built, awaiting live check (PR #12).**
- [ ] **BL-117** [Must] Owner-selected targets (app pools and services) in `blackbox.json` plus install-wizard picker; show "not running" or "not found" with metrics left out, never 0; change the machine-wide busiest list as in BL-125. Selected targets are detected, not typed, where possible (running pools and services offered in the wizard). (source: research, 2026-11-13) **Built, awaiting live check (PR #12).**
- [ ] **BL-118** [Should] SQL DMV sampler: schedulers (VISIBLE ONLINE only, summed in SQL), resource semaphores deltas every 15 s, tempdb page totals every 60 s. Allowed with conditions C1-C14 by the security review. (source: research, 2026-11-13) **Built, awaiting live check (PR #12).**
- [ ] **BL-119** [Should] SQL DMV 60 s sampler: `dm_io_virtual_file_stats` deltas by database_id and file_id (TOP 256, no file paths, no names), AG replica state only when `IsHadrEnabled=1` (no replica server names). Same conditions as BL-118. (source: research, 2026-11-13) **Built, awaiting live check (PR #12).**
- [ ] **BL-120** [Could] Crash-dump file catalogue (name, size, time, target; never the content), checked with the existing 5-minute log scan. Owner ruling 2026-11-13: the Blackbox never creates dumps. (source: research, 2026-11-13) **Built, awaiting live check (PR #12).**
- [ ] **BL-121** [Must] Counter engine rules: English counter names, two baseline samples before any rate, valid-status check on every reading, multi-core CPU not capped at 100. Prerequisite for the counter items. (source: research, 2026-11-13) **Built, awaiting live check (PR #12).**
- [ ] **BL-122** [Must] Blackbox self-budget and self-throttle. Targets: under 0.5% of one core on average, under 50 MB working set, each counter group done within 1 s (else skipped and marked "skipped: slow"), below-normal priority, nothing attached to any target. When over budget: skip optional groups first, then widen the interval to 30 then 60 s, and step back after 10 calm cycles. A benchmark test with fake readers must pass before any new group ships. Counters: add specific counters once and reuse the query handle; re-expand wildcards only when the process set changes. (source: chat 2026-11-13) **Built, awaiting live check (PR #12).**
- [ ] **BL-123** [Must for BL-101/102/118/119] Blackbox changes the SQL review says must come first: SQL runs in its own small service `UnravisBlackboxSql` (same exe, own service account `NT SERVICE\UnravisBlackboxSql`, so a hung SQL call cannot stall host sampling); SQL data goes to a folder only that account, SYSTEM and Administrators can read (today the recordings folder is readable by every local user); fixed error codes only, never driver text; no SQL data over plain HTTP; local SQL only (remote SQL rejected). (source: security review, 2026-11-13) **Partly built (PR #12):** SQL service, own account and fixed error codes exist; the `<folder>\sql` lock and plain-HTTP gaps are listed in `docs/research/blackbox-sql-security-review.md`. Awaiting live check (icacls on a real server).
- [ ] **BL-124** [Must for BL-101/102/118/119] SQL sampler controls C1-C14 as tests: allowlisted fixed statements and a lint test, lock timeout and low deadlock priority, query timeouts, one connection, row caps, back-off and auto-off that survives service restarts, rights-ceiling check (not sysadmin) at every connect, fixed output struct, owner-run grant script and a printed revoke. Full list in `docs/research/blackbox-sql-security-review.md`. (source: security review, 2026-11-13) **Built, awaiting live check (PR #12):** C1-C14 are covered by unit tests; SEC-5 (per-database ceiling) and a real-SQL run are owner-only.
- [ ] **BL-125** [Should] Busiest-process list shape: top 10 by CPU plus top 5 by memory (merged, no duplicates, Idle left out), plus every selected target always listed even when idle. Same CPU cost as today (all processes are read to rank them), smaller files than the current 20. Check Step 5 still finds the web-server row. (source: chat 2026-11-13) **Built, awaiting live check (PR #12).**

Off by default, time-boxed, shows its cost before it starts. No dumps. (source: research, 2026-11-13)

- [ ] **BL-165** Blackbox recorder comes back by itself after an app upgrade or update unless a person stopped it on purpose. Cause: the installer stopped the services and never started them again; a clean stop is not covered by the Windows recovery actions. Fix: Stop writes `stopped-by-user` in the program folder, Start and Remove clear it; the setup plan remembers which services were running and has a "Start the Unravis services again" step (skips a service marked stopped by user). Unit tests only (Linux); needs a Windows upgrade run on a live PC to tick. (2026-11-21)
- [ ] **BL-166** Blackbox "Scan this PC" says "installed, but its settings file could not be read" when the recorder sits in the admin-only `C:\Program Files\Unravis Blackbox`, and offers no "Add it to the list". Workaround: Add one by folder (works). Fix BUILT (PR #50): the recorder keeps a copy of its settings (no key, no secret) in `%ProgramData%\Unravis Blackbox\this-pc.json`, and `ScanThisPC` falls back to that copy when the program folder is unreadable (`internal/blackbox/thispc.go`, `publicconfig.go`). Windows proof owed (an install under the admin-only Program Files folder, then Scan this PC); not ticked. Found on live PC 2026-11-21.

Still unticked. Follow-up 2026-11-22 on `claude/story-refresh` (`web/src/lib/storyReturn.ts`): a change this page gets no signal for (a file added through the case API, another browser tab or client) now shows in Story so far when the person comes back to the page (tab shown or window focused): one quiet read of the case, its files and its health checks, plus the theories on return; at most one per 2 s, none while a read or a move on the case runs or while the page is hidden, none after another tab moved the case; a failed read keeps the story as it was and shows nothing; an answer that says what the page shows is not put in (no redraw), theories included; the newest read of the health checks wins (a check that just finished cannot be dropped by an older, slower list); a theory saved in the drawer while a theories read is on its way is not lost (the read is made once more, never a loop); a case erased in another tab shows as gone on return; a re-read that is thrown away (another case opened on the same page, as after restore as new then Open the new case) takes no health number and the page lets go of the last case's health checks and files at once (unit-pinned; not run end to end: archive is Windows-only on the demo server). Follow-up 2026-11-23 on `claude/story-health-fail`: the page read's failed health read no longer blanks the checks Story so far shows (it puts nothing in and takes no turn; e2e STORY-10 reloads a case whose health read fails and the check stays). Still open: the page read has no newest-wins rule for the case record and file list (a quiet reload that a close, reopen or retry re-read overtakes can still put its older answer over the newer one; a fix must take them only from the newest read that SUCCEEDED, and needs a behavioural test); the health turn counter is shared by all cases, so after a check in case A, opening B and A again, A's own failed health read leaves no checks (never another case's). Unit `web/tests/storyReturn.test.cjs`; e2e STORY-05..09 (a real file added through the demo server's case API, a case erased through it, a theory saved during a held theories read) fail on develop's build or on the first version of this branch; the Story so far panel keeps its x, y and width to the pixel on a return (STORY-03 repaired: it counted the wizard's own pre-send read of the case as the re-read; STORY-04 route hold hardened against a race at test end). Proven on the demo server (a real server on Linux, not the owner's live data); not run on Windows or on live data; a source-only add (Redis, SQL, Mongo, Blackbox) still adds no story line, because tags carry no time; no live push: a change made elsewhere shows on return, not while the page stays in view. Still unticked.
  - Follow-up 2026-11-25 (built, not ticked): page reads, quiet re-reads and return reads now take separate case, file and health turns before their first await. Only a fulfilled answer can win its own kind, so an older held case/file/health response cannot replace a newer successful one, and a newer failure cannot erase a good answer. Opening another case resets all three turns and clears the old files and health immediately. Rendered regression STORY-11 passes on the Linux demo; Windows and live-data acceptance remain owed.

- [ ] **BL-183** `--recover` hardening found in review: (a) RecoverJournal parses the machine journal before it checks the folder, and the folder check follows junctions: check the link and the owner first, then read. (b) The Windows approval prompt started from a hidden process may sit behind other windows: show a small notice that says an approval is waiting. (c) An empty SID on a process counts as the person's in splitApps: treat it as not theirs. **Update 2026-11-21:** (a) done: `RecoverJournal` now checks that the journal exists, that its folder is not a link and that only administrators can change it, before it parses the file (unit test; Windows CI). (c) done 2026-11-21 (owner delegated the call): a program with no reported owner now counts as someone else's, so setup never closes it (the fake list gives new programs the person's SID, as the real Windows list always does for `unravis.exe`). Unit tests; setup tests are Windows-only, CI is the check. (b) not done. **Update 2026-11-22 (a, finish; not ticked):** built in plan PR 4: `JournalPending`, `RecoverJournal` and `Execute` read the machine journal through one function; in the elevated run no byte is read until ProgramData and the journal folder are real folders, the journal folder is administrators-only and the file is proven on the handle it is read from (no link, one name, owner SYSTEM or Administrators, nobody else can write, at most 8 MiB). Any reader error other than "not there" counts as a journal found but refused (kept, reported, never replayed). The journal writer now makes the machine journal with owner Administrators, so the reader accepts what the real writer wrote. Windows CI tests on real NTFS (round trip of the real writer into the real reader, replay, refusals). NOT run on the PC (P2): the real owner of a real journal and a real cut-off, so (a) stays open. **Update 2026-11-22 (b, notice; not ticked):** the OK/Cancel box before the Windows prompt is built in PR 8 (Cancel keeps the journal and the app, note `failed`, exit 3; `--recover --silent` never asks) and is OFF by default: it shows only when `UNRAVIS_RECOVER_NOTICE=1` is set, so `--recover` behaves as before (no box, no wait) and releases are not held for it. The automatic start (`LaunchRecover`) hides the process window, so a box that does not show, or shows behind other windows, would turn every automatic machine recovery into a 10-minute wait and then `failed`. P3 flips it on: run it on nachi's PC with other windows in front (plan P3: OK, Cancel, closed box, No at the prompt) and set `recoverNoticeDefault` to true only after that passes. A refused journal now names its file and says to move it, never delete it. On the CI runner (an elevated administrator) a plain `os.WriteFile` in the administrators-only folder gets owner BA (`O:BAG:S-1-5-21-...-513D:(A;ID;FA;;;SY)(A;ID;FA;;;BA)`), which the reader accepts; this is the runner's configuration, not the PC's (P2).
  - Groomed 2026-11-21 (`docs/pipeline/BL-140-183-groom.md`): (a) PR #70 is half: `JournalPending` still parses before the folder check when elevated; finish slice opens the journal by handle with a size limit. (b) native OK/Cancel notice in `--recover`, before the app closes and before the Windows prompt (PC check). (c) empty SID: small, safe.
  - Status 2026-11-22: (a) merged as #83 (`ReadFileProven`), #105 (read only through it; writer sets owner Administrators; spec S2 owner rule amended: only SYSTEM or Administrators may own the journal, the running account is not trusted). (b) #108 notice: built, off by default (`UNRAVIS_RECOVER_NOTICE=1` turns it on); set the default on only after nachi checks (P3) that the box shows from the hidden `--recover` start. Known limits: a journal with a different owner is refused and kept (the person is told which file to move); `CreateFileSDDL` fallback may drop the owner silently; advice may be cut at 400 bytes on admin runs. Real owner of a real journal (P2) not read yet.

## Desktop app (Tauri) - groomed 2026-11-21 (`docs/desktop/`)

Spec per area: `docs/desktop/spec/`. Items per area: `docs/desktop/groom/` (scope, risks, acceptance). Plan and order: `docs/desktop/PLAN.md`. Nothing here is ticked until it works on the real data path. BL-312 in older text means BL-400 (signing).

- [ ] **BL-301** Go `--pipe-server` mode on a secured named pipe (size ?, ?; see `docs/desktop/groom/01-shell-sidecar.md`)
  - Status 2026-11-22 (not ticked, needs a Windows run and BL-302): code and Linux tests done. `--pipe-server` / `--pipe-only` serve the mux over `pipesec` ui and ag pipes (`internal/pipesrv`). Both pipes pass `X-Audit-Token` unchanged (spec 01 D6); the ag pipe serves only the bridge read routes and `/mcp/v1/`, `/cli/v1/` (D5). Peer PID, image and SID suffix are logged per connection. Linux: `TestPipeOnlyNoListenerNoSessionFile`, `TestServeOverPipeSameJSONAsLoopback` (demo data), `TestPipeRejectsRemoteFlag`, `TestPipeStatsPeerPIDLogged`. Windows CI job `desktop-sidecar` runs `TestServeOverRealPipes`, `TestNoTCPListenerInPipeMode` (GetExtendedTcpTable on the real exe) and `Test-Sidecar.ps1 -Check NoPort` / `-Check Squat`; not yet seen green. Not verified: real cases over the ui pipe equal loopback bytes (live PC), second-user/network denial.
  - Do not release BL-301 without BL-302: pipe mode takes no single-instance gate yet, so it can run beside a browser-mode copy on the same data folder. Nothing launches these flags until BL-302.
  - Spec conflicts to reconcile (the branch follows spec 01 for flags and 02 for the library): pipe library go-winio (01) vs pipesec only (02, used); pipe names `unravis-<role>-<sid8>-<nonce>` (01) vs `unravis.ui` / `unravis.mcp.<32hex>` (02) vs built `unravis.ui.<32hex>` / `unravis.ag.<32hex>`; loopback in pipe mode `--pipe-server --loopback` writes `session.json` (01, built) vs `--pipe-only --preview-loopback` writes none (02 2.20). Spec 02 2.22 (a locator with no token) must give the shell the token, or it cannot call the ui pipe; owner decision needed there.
- [ ] **BL-302** Attach-or-start, singleton and locator file (size M, P1; see `docs/desktop/groom/01-shell-sidecar.md`)
  - Status 2026-11-22 (not ticked, needs a Windows run): `internal/locator` (backend.json v2, atomic owner-only write, `Alive` by pid + creation time + both pipes, `Attach` attach-or-start, `StartDetached`), `internal/appdirs` (run folder), and the pipe-mode singleton in `cmd/audit-explorer` (second copy waits 40 x 250 ms for a live locator and exits 12; owner writes the locator once both pipes serve and removes its own on stop). Demo runs use `backend.demo.json` and the demo mutex. Linux: round trip, no token field, dead/reused pid, unknown version, stale replaced, exit 12, locator write and cleanup. Windows CI (`desktop-sidecar`, not yet seen green): `TestLocatorFileACLOwnerOnly`, `TestSystemProberSelfAndPipes`, `TestTenConcurrentStartsOneOwner`, `TestStaleLocatorReplacedAfterKill`, `Test-Sidecar.ps1 -Check TenStarts|Stale|Acl`. Not verified: any Windows run, two logons (live PC), the CLI/MCP callers (BL-318), `--pipe-server` still has no launcher until BL-306. Fix pass 2026-11-22: the pipe-mode owner now listens on the per-user instance events (`--quit` and a browser-mode second start reach it; Open is only logged until the shell's open call replaces it, D19/BL-306, not built); a pipe-mode copy exits quietly with 11 (elevated, BL-304 number still to agree) or 13 (no live locator in 10 s, for example when the owner is a browser-mode copy, which writes none) and never shows a message box; both pipe names now share one nonce and `Validate` requires it; `PipeExists` fails closed on unexpected errors; `Alive` is documented as existence, not authentication (callers must check the pipe server pid on connect, BL-318); the locator rename waits up to 5 s; `Write` refuses to restrict a run folder that holds non-locator files; the Windows tests wait for killed copies to exit and the ps1 starts copies through `Process.Start` so exit codes are kept. - [ ] **BL-574** Code mode server wiring on top of the broker (BL-363): `/api/code/*` routes, `code-mode.json` settings, case resolver (one open case by default), `OnCaseOpen` call at case open, MCP tools `unravis_types`/`unravis_execute`, `unravis code` CLI (size M, Parallel C). Slice 1 built, see the note at the end. Must carry the grant's source scope, tier, tool list and profile into `RunRequest.Scoped/Sources/Tier/Tools/ProfileID/ProfileRev`, set `Config.Admit` to the dispatcher's `AdmitCodeCall`, cancel a run when its grant is revoked (the dispatcher's `BeginCall` is not used by `AdmitCodeCall`) (the broker refuses surface mcp without them, and a scoped run without a known tier), and `unravis_types` should list only the tools of the grant's tier and should give the run record a field of its own for the scope (today it is the first words of `reason`, built so the caller cannot forge it and the store cannot cut it; cut to 60 characters of source ids). Needs BL-362 and BL-366/367 for a run to start, but routes, settings and refusals can be built and tested first. Also: replay (BL-370) feeds `wire.Ret` frames from the stored blobs. **Slice 1 built (2026-11-25), not ticked:** the HTTP side only. Real and tested: the `code-mode.json` settings file (default off, strict decode, size cap, a damaged file means off, atomic owner-only write, re-read every run); the case resolver (the one open case or the named case, `OnCaseOpen` once per case per process on case open); `GET /api/code/types`, `GET`/`PUT /api/code/settings`, `POST /api/code/run`, `POST /api/code/runs/{id}/cancel` (session token, body caps, strict decode, stable `{code, message}` refusals, MCP bridge refused, 503 until mounted), wired in `cmd/audit-explorer`. Verified on the real sealed sqlite case store: settings, routes, resolver, case lease and erase, refusal paths, recording of a failed run. Verified on the in-memory store only: the orphan-sweep-on-open path (the sealed store sweep is covered in `internal/casestore/sqlite`). Not verified: any script execution, because there is no JS worker yet (Spawner is nil, every run is refused `sandbox_unavailable` and, being refused before `BeginRun`, is not recorded). Remains: the worker, MCP tools `unravis_types`/`unravis_execute`, the `unravis code` CLI, the scoped path (`Config.Admit` from the dispatcher, grant scope into `RunRequest`), cancel on grant revoke, the settings UI, and a way for a client to learn a live run id before it ends. **Required before a worker ships (Opus review of #275, S1 and S3):** end a case's live runs when the case is closed, its key is changed or locked, or it is erased (a `Broker.CancelCase(id)` called before those routes drain, so the lifecycle route neither overtakes a run nor waits on it until `errCaseInUse`), and refuse a run with 423 while the master key is locked or damaged, with tests that start a blocked run and then close, rekey, lock and erase the case. Not exploitable today because no script can start. Smaller: the run body is buffered before the run slot is checked (accepted: capped at 6 times the 256 KiB script limit plus 8 KiB, about 1.5 MiB per request, and only the app's own session token reaches the route; revisit if the route is ever opened to more callers). `Load` opens the file, then checks the same handle (built earlier), and on Windows now reads the access list back from that handle (BL-574 remainder, 2026-11-25): a file whose list gives access to anyone but the owner's account, SYSTEM and Administrators, or that has no list, is treated as damaged (code mode off until the owner saves again); tested on Windows only (`TestLoadRefusesAFileWithAWidenedAccessList`, fails without the change), the Linux build keeps the mode-bit check. **Slice 2a built (2026-11-25), not ticked:** the case lifecycle safety the Opus review of #275 asked for. A live code run is ended, and awaited for up to 5 seconds, before a case is closed, its key is set, changed or removed, it is archived or erased, or the master key locks; a run is refused while the case's exclusive slot is held (`acquireReadIdle`); `POST /api/code/run` answers 423 while the master key is locked or damaged. Tested with a blocked fake worker that is ended by close, erase and master-key lock (each test fails without the change). Still open in slice 2: MCP tools, grant scope, cancel on revoke, CLI, settings UI, worker. **Slice 2b built (2026-11-25), not ticked:** the MCP surface and grant scope. Real and tested (fake worker, demo repository and a real sealed case; nothing on live data): `unravis_types` returns declarations for only the grant's tier, tool list and sources; `unravis_execute` runs through the broker for the calling grant (never the loopback session-token route) with the grant's sources, tier, tool list and profile in the run request, and cannot be widened by script text or tool arguments (unknown arguments are refused, a call outside the list ends the run); an unknown tier, a scoped run with no sources or tools, and surface mcp without a scope are refused; both tools are refused with the stable codes when code mode is off, and 401 when the grant is revoked; `Broker.CancelGrant` ends a grant's runs on revoke (blocked fake worker, row ends `cancelled`); main binds the broker's admitter to the dispatcher (`AdmitCodeCall`) and `OnRevoke` to `CancelGrant`. NOT done: no JS worker, so a run ends `sandbox_unavailable` in the app; per-grant code permission is built (BL-574 M1; the owner's code-mode switch is the second gate); operator `AllowIdentifiers` stays false, so record ids never reach a code run in production (tested only with the test switch); no production MCP stdio or agent entrance advertises the two tools yet (only `mcpagent` tests and `mcp.ServeCaller(withCode)` do), so a real MCP host cannot reach `unravis_execute` end to end; the run record has no separate scope field (the begin record is hash-chained in a sealed store; the scope is in the `[sources: ...; tier: ...]` note at the front of `reason`); the CLI and the settings UI are still open. **Required before a worker ships (Opus review of 2b):** (M1) is built: code mode is its own permission of a grant (spec D7), default off everywhere, set by an "Allow code mode" checkbox on the approval card (separate from the identifiers choice), never inherited by a re-approval or reissue, and `unravis_execute` and `unravis_types` refuse a grant without it (denied, code `code_not_granted`, nothing runs); (L1) L1 built: a code run is counted in the same limiter as the host's other calls (class `code`: one run at once per host, two in all, 30 an hour, and its result bytes go to the ten-minute byte budget); a call refused before a script started (a gate, or a broker refusal such as code mode off or no sandbox) does not use up a run; result bytes are charged on top of the bytes of the script's own tool calls, so a script that returns what it read is charged for both. Store v5 is one-way: an older binary refuses it with `ErrNewerVersion` and keeps the store (see BL-353). **CLI review (PR #296):** `unravis code run` is reachable by any process the pipe lets in as a human (peer image name only; `IsMCPImage == IsCLIImage`), so a shell-capable agent on the same user session could run unscoped code once the owner switched code mode on: owner decision needed before a worker ships. Built in the BL-574 remainder (2026-11-25): a test that fails if `main.go` passes a nil code broker to the CLI handler (`TestMainGivesTheCodeBrokerToTheCLIHandler`); `TIMEOUT`/`CANCELED` from `unravis code run` now name the run id the broker sealed ("The run was recorded in the case as ..."), so its record can be found (the logs stay in the case record; the error answer has no data field). M1 review follow-ups: the tools/list entrance advertises unravis_types and unravis_execute per server (`withCode`), not per grant, so once a production caller of `ServeCaller` exists a grant without code mode will see both tools and get `denied` on use (advertise per grant); the approval card and row copy must change when the JS worker and the code-mode settings switch ship.

- [ ] **BL-353** Update and rollback with MCP clients attached (size S, P4; see `docs/desktop/groom/03-mcp.md`) OWED (BL-574 M1): the MCP audit store is now schema v5 and one-way. A rolled-back (older) binary refuses it with `ErrNewerVersion` and does not set the store aside, so MCP grants are unavailable on the old binary and nothing in `internal/update` or `internal/setup` knows the store version. The rollback must either warn that MCP grants are unavailable on the old version, or back up the store before an update and restore it on rollback. Both built 2026-11-25, not ticked (Linux tests; owner ruling: implement both): the first open of an older store keeps `mcp.db.v<old>.bak` (made once, never replaced; no copy means no upgrade, `ErrBackup`); a build that meets a newer store leaves it untouched and the AI access screen says `newer`; the downgrade note says AI programs cannot connect until the newer version is back. Open: a real N to N-1 rollback run on Windows, and handing the copy back to an older build is manual.

The settings control for it is prototype-only until nachi approves. **"No time limit" backend 2026-11-26 (built, not ticked, needs the Opus security review):** `cliUnlimited` on `GET/PUT /api/code/settings`, memory only like the window (shut at app start, when code mode goes off, and by any timed window; never in `code-mode.json`; exclusive with `cliMinutes` above 0), each window change one app-log line; the UI is not built (prototype v2 only). Limits kept after the Opus review: the gate is shut whenever code mode is seen off (a settings read, a run, or a save that switches it on from off), but an off-then-on edit of the file by a same-user program with no GET, run or PUT in between leaves an open gate open; and any holder of the session token, the command line included, can open the gate, so the consent step is not a boundary against the same user.

  - `app status` is built with BL-385. Built, not ticked (this slice): `app start --headless` and `app stop`. Owner side: `--owner=headless|shell` on the app program (pipe mode only; hello says it; the thin command line refuses the flag like the other app options), `app.stop` in `clisrv` (stops only an owner that says `headless`; any other owner answers PERMISSION_DENIED "quit it from the tray"; the quit starts 100 ms after the answer is written, through the same cancel as `--quit`), and the owner refuses an `app.start` or `doctor` op (the command line's own work). CLI side: `cmdtree/appctl.go` (start: attach first, so a running app is `alreadyRunning` and nothing is started on an app that cannot be read or trusted; else start through `IO.StartHeadless`, poll hello every 100 ms for 15 s, the exit code of the child maps 11/12/13 to ELEVATION_REFUSED/OWNER_BUSY/UPSTREAM_UNAVAILABLE; `started` is true only when the owner that answers is the child; stop: PERMISSION_DENIED for a non-headless owner before anything is sent, waits 5 s for the pid to go, an app that is not running is `alreadyStopped`, exit 0), `cliattach/start_windows.go` (`<install>\unravis.exe --pipe-server --owner=headless`, detached, no window, no stdio, retry without job breakaway). Tested on Linux with a fake starter and the in-memory owner (every branch above; mutant checks). The Windows start is only compile-checked (`GOOS=windows go vet`). Opus review fixes: stop waits for the process to end, exit 12 attaches to the other owner (`alreadyRunning`), the child's environment is cleaned (no `AUDIT_*`, `UNRAVIS_*`; `LOCALAPPDATA` from the known folder), the real `--owner` wiring is tested, `--request {}` works, exit 13 text. Follow-ups left: the desktop shell does not pass `--owner=shell` yet (area 01, `desktop/src-tauri/src/locator.rs`), so hello says `app` for it (the stop is refused either way); no warning when the start runs inside a job that ends it, or for an elevated shell; `app stop` reports `alreadyStopped` for a browser-mode Unravis (it writes no locator). Owner lease built, not ticked: `internal/ownerlease` (D19; `owner.lock` / `owner-demo.lock` in the run folder, `CreateFile` share mode 0, taken in `enterPipeSingleton` after the singleton gate (which includes `refuseElevated`) and before the pipe listeners; busy is exit 12 and the gate is released; the locator folder check lets the two lock files stay). Tests: `TestLeaseExclusive`, `TestLeaseFreedOnKill` (real child process killed), `TestAcquireCreatesRunDir`, `TestPipeModeLeaseBusyExits12`, `TestPipeModeLeaseOtherErrorIsFatal`, `TestPipeModeLeaseReleasedOnExit`, and the real-exe tests that start `--pipe-only` with the lock file in the run folder. Checked by hand on this PC: with another process holding `owner-demo.lock` open exclusively, `unravis.exe --pipe-only --demo` exits 12 with the message and starts again after the holder closes. Not verified: the actual console plus RDP case (two sign-in sessions of one user), a real start and stop on Windows, two concurrent starts, an elevated shell (owed to nachi, needs a second session).

- [ ] **BL-425** Go sidecar idle power policy (EcoQoS and fallback) (size ?, ?; see `docs/desktop/groom/07-performance.md`)
  - Built 2026-11-25, not ticked: `internal/power` applies the 30-second activity grace and checks every 10 seconds only while normal. Authenticated UI/MCP requests touch and begin the policy; unauthorized requests do not. In-flight requests, case-file streaming and updater download/install busy state block throttling, and update downloads/installs wake the policy before work starts. An activity generation check restores normal power if activity begins during an idle transition. Windows uses current-process EcoQoS on build 16299+, retries `EXECUTION_SPEED` only on `ERROR_INVALID_PARAMETER`, falls back to below-normal priority on Server 2016, and rolls back partial idle changes without touching WebView2 children. Unit, server, fake-Windows and cross-build checks pass; Windows CI runs this package. A failed idle apply is followed by one restore to normal; if that fails too the mode stays idle and the next request retries. The forced memory return after a request over 5 seconds runs at most once every 30 seconds. Live proof still owed: Windows 11 Efficiency Mode after 30 seconds, first post-idle request within 200 ms, and Server 2016 below-normal priority.

- [ ] **BL-431** Chart client: binary series fetch and debounced zoom/pan (size ?, ?; see `docs/desktop/groom/07-performance.md`)
  - Built 2026-11-25, not ticked: case series support opt-in `format=binary` with the URS1 prefix, bounded padded JSON header and eight-double points (min/mean/max/count/first/last/q; absent optional values are NaN), with strict decoding and one JSON retry for malformed binary answers. Blackbox follows the repository's media-type negotiation and plain-u32 framing while preserving its six-double M4 points. Both clients send visible width, debounce zoom/pan reads by 150 ms, cancel held reads, suppress stale answers and retain bounded request-to-paint samples in `window.__unravisPerf`. Server, unit and rendered regression checks pass for framing, pixel parity, timing, cancellation and fallback. Lite/live-data and Windows acceptance remain owed.

- [ ] **BL-450** Fuzz targets: PR corpus replay and nightly runs (size M, P3; see `docs/desktop/groom/08-testing-repo.md`) **Scrubber targets added 2026-11-23 (not ticked):** `internal/secretscrub/fuzz_test.go` (`FuzzMaskCanary` plants a secret in seventeen forms after arbitrary text and fails if it survives; `FuzzMaskBounds` checks time, growth, `Carry`; with the older `FuzzMask`) and the nightly matrix. First local runs (about 6 million inputs each) found two real leaks, now fixed with tests and committed seeds: a stray quote before a tag hid the next `<add key="password" ...>`, and `-pW,` read the next line as its value (the first fix for each was too broad: the fresh review found it hid tags with a `<` in a value and lists with an unquoted comma; both reworked and covered by tests). The old `-P,` then `-P x` gap was fixed 2026-11-24 (not ticked): the scanner keeps a tool's short-option context across a comma-wrapped newline, with ordinary and CRLF continuation tests, a non-continuation guard and a fake canary committed to `FuzzMaskCanary`; local fuzz passed 6,139,217 executions. Still open from BL-450: the Rust target, and the live-PC run on a real update manifest and MCP log.
  - Built 2026-11-23, Go part (not ticked): `FuzzParseManifest`, `FuzzVerify`, `FuzzVerifyControl` (`internal/update/fuzz_test.go`; Verify cases sign the fuzzed bytes with a fixed test key so the fuzzer reaches the parser and field checks), `FuzzLocatorRead` (`internal/locator`), `FuzzBridgeServe` (`internal/mcp`; output must be one JSON-RPC object per line, each under 1 MiB, and the loop must stop at end of input). A plain `go test` replays the seeds on every pull request. `.github/workflows/nightly-fuzz.yml` runs each target 2 minutes every night (02:23 UTC) and uploads a crasher file as an artifact. A planted-bug target (`-tags fuzzseed`, `FuzzSeededBug`) proves a crash is found: locally the fuzzer finds it in 1.5 s and writes the crasher; the workflow's "seeded" input runs the same on GitHub (not run there yet). Each real target ran 20 s locally with no crash. Left: the Rust fuzz targets (`desktop/ipc-core`, need the nightly toolchain), `FuzzHTTPOverPipeRequestLine` (no parser exposed), the live-PC run seeded with the real feed manifest and a real MCP session log, the committed-crasher replay proof.

- [ ] **BL-540** [Low, security] (found in the review of BL-452) `start_unix_nanos` in `desktop/ipc-core/src/locator.rs` is looser than the Go writer: it accepts signed fields (`T-1:00:00Z`, because `i64::parse` takes `+` and `-`) and impossible dates such as 31 February. The start time ties the locator record to a process, so refuse both (digits only, days per month) and add the cases to `tests/boundaries.rs`. **Built 2026-11-23 (not ticked):** fields are digits only at the exact widths Go writes (year 4, others 2), a dot needs digits after it, seconds 0 to 59 and the day must exist in its month (leap years too); cases in `tests/boundaries.rs`; `cargo mutants --in-diff` on the change: 33 mutants, 33 caught. Not checked against a live locator file from a running Windows host.

Waived in `desktop/osv-scanner.toml` (the ignore decision was taken by the coordinator session, nachi was not asked; revert the file and the `osv` step if unwanted) (the scanner reads the config next to the scanned lock file; a root file was tried and ignored) with the reason and an expiry date of 2027-02-23 each, and the osv step in `supply-chain.yml` is blocking again, which makes the check red and gates nothing (master requires only `build`; no retry for an osv.dev outage). A new step in that job runs `cargo tree` and fails if a waived crate ever shows up in the Windows build. The earlier BL-460 note that the two `internal/store` data races were "not looked at": they were already gone (see BL-441a; 30 runs of both tests under `-race` here were clean). Groups 2 and 3 fixed in PR claude/uifix-batch3: the hour squares of the Daily and hourly view are 24 px tall buttons that paint their 20 px square in a `::before` (a negative margin keeps the 20 px row pitch; on a phone or touch the squares are 24 x 24 and the grid scrolls sideways inside its box), and the two zoom slider handles are 24 px wide with the same 4 px grip (the grab zone of a mouse press grew from 6 to 12 px each side, as on touch). Pixel-identical on desktop (1440 and 1100 px, dark and light, rest, hover and focus of the squares, the zoom handles and the legend) against develop; spec UIFIX-13 compares screenshots with the old rules put back. (8 px wide). Desktop does not move, not even the keyboard focus ring: the Review link, the update bar link and the counts keep their box and get the 24 px hit area from an absolutely positioned `::after` (3 px above and below, 8 px each side for the counts), and the audit now counts such a pseudo-element for the tap target size (`hitArea` in `tools/uiaudit/lib/page-checks.js`, with a self-test). Only the setup rows change their box (the label's own box, 2.58 px of padding and an equal negative margin; their focus ring is the checkbox's, which does not move). Local full demo-build audit (all 512 looks, 0 new errors, 0 new warnings): 68 known small-target warnings before, 18 after, all 18 of them the BL-492 entries; the seven group-4 baseline entries are removed. Guard: `tools/e2e/specs/ui-layout.spec.ts` UIFIX-14a to 14c and 14f (hit area of 24 x 24 px or more with presses at its corners landing on the control, nothing else moves against the old rules, focus rings and rest pictures equal to the old rules: byte for byte for the update link and the counts, within 8 pixels and 8 of 255 colour levels for the rounded ring of the Review link; 14a to 14c fail on develop). - [x] **BL-493** Heatmap legend squares render grey (CSS order bug), fix as its own change (found in the PR for BL-475 groups 2 and 3 review, 2026-11-22): in the Daily and hourly view the four "Lower ... Higher" squares under the hour grid (`.hm-swatch` in `HourHeatmap.svelte`) are all grey, not the four heat colours of the squares above. Cause: `.level-1` to `.level-4` set the heat colours, but the later `.hm-swatch` rule (`background: var(--surface-2)`) has the same specificity and wins, so the legend never shows the scale. Seen on develop at 1440 and 1100 px, dark and light. Fix: scope the level colours to `.hm-swatch.level-N` (a visible desktop change: 576 px per screenshot). Not fixed in the BL-475 PR on purpose, because that PR must leave desktop pixel-identical; UIFIX-11a there pins the grey legend, so this fix must update that spec in the same change. Demo-verified only. Fixed in the claude/uifix-batch5 pull request: `.hm-swatch.level-1` to `.level-4` set the heat colours after the grey `.hm-swatch` rule (an intended visible desktop change, only the four legend squares; the grid and every other pixel of the legend are unchanged, UIFIX-13a compares them against the old rules with only these four colours added). Browser spec UIFIX-15 (dark and light) checks each legend square against the colour of an hour square of its level (a copy of a real square when the demo data has none of that level); it fails on develop and passes now, local run. UIFIX-13a no longer pins the grey legend. Audit re-run pending (CI); demo-verified only.

- [ ] **BL-495f** (BUILT in PR #250, not ticked, awaits the owner's live check) `?` popovers open on click only, not on hover or focus. Decision (2026-11-23, taken without the owner): the `?` hint of a technical word (`Hint.svelte`) opens on a click and on Enter or Space with the focus on the button (that is the button's own click), takes the focus into the popover, closes on Esc (focus back on the button), on Tab past either end, and on a click or focus anywhere else; it never opens on hover or on focus alone (touch cannot hover, and a popover that opens when the focus only passes by would pull the focus from a person tabbing through the page). Read of the code first: everything but the last point already held, so the build pins it (spec HINTKEY-01 to 04 in `tools/e2e/specs/ui-hint-keys.spec.ts`, `web/tests/hint.keys.test.cjs`) and adds `aria-haspopup="dialog"` to the trigger. The `?` of HelpNote and the `i` of FieldHelp are inline disclosures (a button toggling a block in the page), unchanged. Demo-verified only (Linux).

It is not a Blackbox view but the case's Resource counters (card U1 of `docs/superpowers/plans/2026-10-25-importer-wave2-plan.md`: mounted in `CaseTools` below the Timeline; the Go side is live: `GET /api/cases/{id}/resource` and `/series`, `internal/resource`, the rules RC1 to RC9, the `win-counters`, `sql-pdh`, `dotnet-counters`, `redis-info` and `win-live` importers feed it). Now `CaseHome.svelte` mounts it (lazy, between Health checks and Sources and connections) only when the case holds counter files (`resourceFiles(imports)` in `lib/resourceSpan.ts`: the importers that are sources of `internal/resource/metrics.go`, a web test keeps the two lists equal): a case with only IIS or app logs mounts nothing, reads nothing from `/resource`, draws nothing and shifts nothing (review of PR 253: the first build drew a "These files cover" offer for log-only cases and a "Reading..." flash that broke STORY-07). It gets the case, the audit window on screen (`scope.query`), the zone, the period the counter files cover (`caseSpan(counterFiles)`, for "Show that period") and the API P95 of the analysis on screen, and keeps its own window (`ownWindow`: its Zoom buttons do not move the analysis zoom, which is not on screen on the case page). It is read again, and an open host closes, when the number of kept counter files changes. A 63-character host name wraps inside the block on a phone (grid tracks `minmax(0, 1fr)`, `overflow-wrap`). The intro line no longer repeats the plain help line under it. Only Redis and SQL Server names drop a trailing dot; Blackbox names are only lower-cased. Known and left: after a send the hub moves to Sources, so focus ends on the page body; if the case read fails the send still goes ahead (the server refuses a closed case itself); a screen reader may read the "Checking this case again" line only once, not tested with one. WIZG-02b and WIZG-05 are regression checks that also pass on develop; the reset blocker is proven by the unit test only.** BL-148 stays unticked: the fourth leftover (a Blackbox recorder saved before the run is not undone) is not built.
