## Daily security and performance sweep 2026-11-21 (read-only code review; nothing built, nothing ticked)

Scope: the whole tree (history starts inside the 7-day window), weighted to the update chain, setup, the Blackbox service and the app's timers. Each item was checked by reading the code; none was run (Windows-only paths are not run on Linux). Ids use `SWP-` so parallel branches do not collide on `BL-` numbers; renumber when merging. Ranked most severe first.

- [ ] **SWP-1021-1** [Medium, security] Setup locks the Blackbox folders by path, not by handle, and never checks who owns them or whether they are links. `blackbox.Manager.Install` relies on `OSEnv.SecureDir` (opens the folder without following a link, refuses a folder owned by another account, sets owner and ACL through that handle; `internal/blackbox/service_secure_windows.go:84-143`) and on `SecureCreator`/`FolderGuard`. The setup program's `bbEnv` (`internal/setup/plan.go:570-585`) implements neither: its `SecureDir` calls `Txn.SetSDDL` -> `osSecurity.SetSDDL` -> `SetNamedSecurityInfo(path, owner=BA, protected DACL)` (`internal/setup/os_windows.go:532-542`), which follows a junction. The preflight for the recordings folder only does `os.Stat` (`internal/setup/preflight.go:337-352`). The default recordings folder is a predictable drive-root name, `D:\UnravisBlackbox` (`internal/blackbox/config.go:28`), where ordinary users can usually create folders. `PublishThisPC` also does `os.MkdirAll(%ProgramData%\Unravis Blackbox, 0o755)` and only then locks it (`internal/blackbox/publicconfig.go:44-47`), so the same applies there. Failure scenario: a standard local user creates `D:\UnravisBlackbox` as a junction to a folder they cannot read (another account's profile, an app folder); an administrator later installs the Blackbox through Unravis setup; the elevated worker sets Administrators as owner and a protected ACL with Users read/execute on the junction target and inherits it down the tree, so the target's files become readable by every local user (or its ACL is broken), and the recorder writes into it. Proposed fix: make `bbEnv.SecureDir` journal the old SDDL and then call the handle-based `blackbox.OSEnv{}.SecureDir` (no-follow, owner check); implement `SecureCreator` (create with the ACL in the create call) and `FolderGuard` on `bbEnv`; in `checkRecordingsFolder` use `Lstat`, refuse a link and a folder owned by a non-administrator; in `PublishThisPC` create the folder with the ACL in the create call (as `MkdirSDDL` does) instead of `MkdirAll` then lock. Add a Windows test with a junction as the recordings folder. Needs a Windows run to tick.
- [ ] **SWP-1021-2** [Low-Medium, performance] The silent updater re-hashes the whole setup program every minute while a failure stays on screen. `autoStep` (`internal/server/updater_auto.go:167`) evaluates `u.fileStillThere(m)` (reads and SHA-256s the downloaded setup file, tens of MB) before it checks `u.autoFailed()`. When the person's own "Apply now" failed (state stays `failed`, `autoFailed()` false), every 1-minute tick reads the file and then returns. It stops only when the state changes. Fix: test `u.autoFailed()` first (a bool), and call `fileStillThere` only when it is true; or remember the check result per `(version, state)`. **Checked 2026-11-23 (not ticked):** already mitigated in the code: `fileStillThere` remembers an intact file for 10 minutes per version (`fileOKFor`/`fileOKAt`, test in `updater_auto_test.go`), so a failure that stays on screen costs one read of the setup file every 10 minutes, not every minute. Not changed further: the person's failed "Apply now" with a missing file must still fall through to a new download, which needs the check.
- [ ] **SWP-1021-3** [Low, performance] The always-on recorder reads the whole new program into memory to hash it. `fileSHA256Hex` uses `os.ReadFile` (`internal/blackbox/selfupdate.go:501-507`), and the staged program may be up to `maxProgramSize` = 200 MiB (`selfupdate.go:42`), against the Blackbox budget of 50 MB working set (BL-122). One-off per update, but it lands in the service that must stay small. Fix: hash from the open file with `io.Copy` into `sha256.New()` (`stageFromZip` already streams the copy). Same pattern, not always-on, lower priority: `LaunchRollback` reads the whole old setup to look for a text marker (`internal/update/launch_windows.go:60`; `update.SetupCanRecover` already does this in 1 MB pieces) and `setupCanBeRolledBackTo` reads it whole during an upgrade (`internal/setup/plan.go:941-951`). **Built 2026-11-23 (not ticked):** `fileSHA256Hex` already streams with `io.Copy` (test `TestFileSHA256HexOfALargeFile`, 8 MiB). The two secondary readers now stream too: `update.FileHasAll` (1 MiB pieces, one piece in memory, finds a text across a piece end) is used by `LaunchRollback`, `setupCanBeRolledBackTo` and `SetupCanRecover`. Linux unit tests; the Windows-only launch path compiles (`GOOS=windows go vet`) but has not run on a PC.
- [ ] **SWP-1021-4** [Low, network] A feed that fails a trust check is retried at full rate forever. A rejected result (expired manifest, bad signature) carries no `Backoff` (`internal/update/fetch.go:330`), so the app and every standalone recorder check again at the normal 30-minute cadence, each time fetching both files and making one retry after 3 s (4 requests per check, no ETag because the cache is filled only after a good verify). The expired-feed case is the known "frozen fleet" risk (OTA research). Fix: set `Backoff: true` on a rejected result after the retry (the existing doubling, capped at 6 h), so a broken or expired feed costs the fleet little; the first good feed then resumes within the cap, and Check for updates in the app still runs at once. **Checked 2026-11-23 (not ticked):** already built: a rejected result sets `Backoff: true` (`internal/update/fetch.go`), test `TestRejectedAnswerAsksForBackoff` (expired manifest). Not seen on a live feed.
- [ ] **SWP-1021-5** [Low, design to confirm with the owner] The signed control file (pause, withdraw, staged rollout) fails open on the internet path. `fetchControl` returns nil on any fetch or verify problem and `applyControl` holds a release only when a live, remembered control file says so (`internal/update/fetch.go:393-410`, `433-446`; only the folder path sets `required`). A machine that has never seen a control file, or whose remembered one expired (up to 90 days), installs a withdrawn or paused release when the control file cannot be fetched (a blocked or rate-limited host, a network that drops one URL). It cannot install anything unsigned; the effect is that a withdrawal does not reach every machine. Options: accept and document; or hold for a short grace window (for example 24 h after the manifest's `published` time) when no live control file has ever been read for the channel, then fail open. Owner decision.
- [ ] **SWP-1021-6** [Low, performance] `Updater.Status()` reads the update-result file from disk on every call while it holds `u.mu` (`internal/server/updater.go:205-209`, and again in `resultIsFromThisLaunch`, line 376). Every screen poll pays a disk read and blocks the check loop and the minute tick (`availableManifest` takes the same lock) if that disk is slow. Fix: read the file outside the lock, or cache it by file modification time. **Checked 2026-11-23 (not ticked):** already built: `Updater.Status()` reads the result file before it takes `u.mu` (`internal/server/updater.go`). No slow-disk test.

Checked and found sound (no item): signed manifest and control parsing (strict, signature before parsing, channel and key role, rollback and same-seq pins), download hash and size on write and again from disk, hand-off to the installer (file held open while hashed and started; setup holds its own file open and the elevated worker repeats the check), the Blackbox self-update path (package name, program hash from the signed manifest, `StartVerified`), the elevated journal replay (BL-183 a: link and owner checked before parse), the recorder's HTTP server (key before throttle, bounded failure table, connection caps, no-follow file open), and the workflows (actions pinned by commit, inputs passed through `env`).

Status 2026-11-22: SWP-1021-1 fixed and merged (PR 102, setup locks folders through a no-follow handle; its Windows tests ran in CI, a real setup run on the owner's PC is not done yet). SWP-1021-2, -3, -4 and -6 fixed and merged (PR 101, unit-tested, not checked on live data). SWP-1021-5 decided: the control file stays fail-open for new withdrawals but keeps the last known withdrawals; written down in `docs/auto-updater/CONTROL.md`. Nothing here is ticked, because none of it has run live yet.

## Security sweep 2026-11-22: release manager and supply chain (PR #166; none ticked until the live checks below are done)

- [ ] **SWP-1122-1** [Medium, security] (sweep id SUP-1) A promotion merged whatever head `promote/*` had at merge time. Built in PR #166 (`tools/release/Promote-Develop.ps1`, `ReleaseLib.ps1`): the run records the one head it may merge (the `DIST_COMMIT` sha, else the branch head after master was merged in), stops with a clear message when the pull request head differs (3 reads 5 s apart, so a late GitHub update is not a false alarm), requires the web files commit to sit directly on the checked head, and merges with `--match-head-commit` on that value. A reused branch is judged at the commit read first. A "Merge master into promote/<sha7>" commit is accepted only after the clean-merge check (`Test-CleanMasterMerge`, the rule `Get-PromotionSideCommits` uses; it fetches master and the branch into a throw-away bare repo, needs git 2.38 or later). Verified: offline tests in `Test-ReleaseManager.ps1` (head check, lag and persistent mismatch, merge-in accepted and refused cases on real git repos, source shape of the script); the new tests fail on the old code. Not verified: a real promotion on GitHub (merges API 201 parent check, compare by sha, the token header fetch), so it stays unticked. Optional later: a ruleset limiting pushes to `promote/*`.
- [ ] **SWP-1122-3** [Low, security] (sweep id SUP-3) `Make-Keys.ps1` built the key tool in the working tree without neutralising Go settings. Built in PR #166: it loads `ReleaseLib.ps1` (`GOFLAGS`, `GOWORK`, `GOENV`, `GOTOOLCHAIN`) and builds from a detached worktree of `HEAD`; `New-ToolWorktree` now removes its worktree when the build fails (also for `Set-Control.ps1` and `Refresh-Feed.ps1`). Verified: offline tests (AST order, env neutralised in a child shell, a broken commit leaves no registered worktree); a reviewer ran the success path on Linux up to the passphrase prompt. Not verified: a full run on the owner's Windows PC with real passphrases.
- [ ] **SWP-1122-4** [Low, supply chain] (sweep id SUP-4) The npm policy and the Dependabot cooldown covered only `web/`. Built in PR #166: `web/.npmrc` copied to `tools/screens`, `tools/uiaudit`, `tools/e2e`; `cooldown: { default-days: 3 }` on every Dependabot entry. Verified: `npm ci` in copies of `tools/screens` and `tools/uiaudit` passes with npm 10.9.9 and 11.21.3; `dependabot.yml` parses. Not verified: the cooldown on a real Dependabot run. Open: `min-release-age` is active only with npm 11.10 or later and `supply-chain.yml` checks it in `web` only; `tools/uiaudit` has no Dependabot entry; `tools/e2e` has no dependencies or lockfile yet.

## Security and performance sweep 2026-11-22: Releases app, scrubbers, web limits, performance (PRs #164, #165, #167; none ticked until the live checks below are done)

Full report: `sweeps/sweep-2026-11-22-security-perf.md` in the project folder. Each item was reproduced by a Go test, curl or pwsh before it was fixed; each fix has tests that fail on the old code and a fresh Opus review.

- [ ] **SWP-1122-2** [Medium, security + performance] (sweep ids REL-1 to REL-4, and the dashboard poll cost) Releases app. Built in PR #164: (REL-1) stop reasons in `state.json` and `audit.jsonl` are redacted line by line before they are cut (`internal/releasedash/local.go` `redactText`), and `Release.ps1` `Fail` stores a redacted reason (`Protect-ReasonText`); (REL-3) Go test names are redacted; (REL-4) the Redactor and its PowerShell twin hide `--passphrase <v>`, `--pin=<v>`, `*_PASS=`, `*_PIN=`, `key_seed`, `user:pass@` in URLs and `curl -u` / `--user`, with explicit character classes so both agree; (REL-2 and performance) `internal/releasedash/memo.go` keeps read logs and CI test reports (least recently used, one shared read per file, panic safe, big CI logs limited to 2 reads at once), and `Release()` reads each run log once per page. Measured on Linux: 30k-line run log, first poll 0.9 s, five more polls 11 ms total (before: about 3.5 s CPU per poll on 20k lines; a 64 MiB CI log took 22 s and 1.4 GB per `/api/log` page). **Not verified:** a real release stop on Windows through the new `Fail` path, a live Releases window on a real rc.
- [ ] **SWP-1122-5** [Medium, security] (sweep ids OTA-1, OTA-2) The secret scrubbers for case reports and findings exports (`internal/evidence/secretscrub.go`) and the app and setup logs (`internal/applog/sanitize.go`, `internal/setup/log.go`) stopped a password at the first space (`Password=correct horse battery` kept ` horse battery`). Built in PR #167: an unquoted value runs to `;` or end of line, quotes and braces honour their escapes, an empty value takes the next line. Trade-off, accepted: free text after an unquoted password on the same line is removed too. **Not verified:** a real export or log from a live case; no caller logs a full connection string today.
- [ ] **SWP-1122-6** [Low, security] (sweep ids WEB-1, WEB-2) A web page could spend the shared per-minute limit of `/launch` and `/api/session-proof` with `<img>` requests, so the real start link answered 404 and the MCP bridge got 429. Built in PR #165: requests a browser marks as a sub-resource, a prefetch or a page request are refused before the limit counts them. Measured with Chromium (the headers `Sec-Fetch-Dest: image` / `empty`, and `document` with `Sec-Fetch-Site: none` for a real navigation). **Not verified:** Edge on Windows, the tray opener. Not fixed on purpose: a local process (not a browser) can still use up the limit.
- [ ] **SWP-1122-7** [Low, accepted] (sweep id REL-5) The Releases dashboard session code is reusable for the life of the process, appears on the command line and its cookie is not port scoped. Not changed: reload and the UI audit rely on re-using it, the tool is single user on Windows. Revisit if it runs on a shared host.
- [ ] **SWP-1122-8** [Low-medium, supply chain, owner decision] govulncheck and osv-scanner gate nothing: `supply-chain.yml` runs them only on the promotion PR and master and the Go scans (BL-460) and the osv scan (BL-460a) are blocking since 2026-11-23, but the check is red-only: nothing waits on it (see BL-460) (mongo-driver GO-2026-5327, Go standard library `net/http`). Fix is BL-460, then remove `continue-on-error`; a wait in `Promote-Develop.ps1` would gate nothing before that. Also for the owner: check in the GitHub settings that `build` and the supply-chain jobs are required checks on master (this session's token cannot read branch protection).
- [ ] **SWP-1122-9** [info] Performance and footprint, 2026-11-22 (demo app on Linux, real HTTP import path): 200k, 1M and 5M IIS lines import at 27k, 10.1k and 8.2k rows/s (Windows baseline 11.5k and 9.1k: same band), peak RSS 173, 241 and 263 MiB (memory stays flat with 5x the input), idle CPU 0%, 116 MiB idle RSS in the demo, start to link 0.5 s. Not measured: the Blackbox recorder, the live sampler and the local reader (Windows only); run `internal/perfbench` on the owner's PC to refresh those.
- [ ] **SWP-1122-10** [Medium, security] (follow-up to SWP-1122-5, PR #180) The secret scrubbers (case reports and exports, app log, setup log) now also catch backslash-escaped quotes, colon and prefixed keys (`DB_PASSWORD=`, `MYSQL_PWD=`, `password: x`), XML and web.config forms, JSON numbers, and slog attribute keys, groups and `http.Header`. Tested on Linux with table tests and 256 KB hostile-input timing; a fresh review found no regression against develop on about 300 inputs. **Not verified:** a real export or log from a live case; Windows. **Still leaks (found by the review, not fixed here, none new):** command-line `--password x` / `-pX` / `sqlcmd -P`; YAML block scalars; JSON inside a string; non-ASCII colon or keys; web.config pairs whose key is not a password (`ClientSecret`, `ApiKey`, `StorageAccountKey`, `AuthToken`); app-log slog maps other than `http.Header` / `map[string][]string` / `map[string]string` (`url.Values`, `map[string]any`, struct fields); an escaped quote in a `secret`/`api_key` value in applog and setup; an unterminated quoted secret in the export scrubber; setup log has no Bearer, Basic, api_key or URL-credential rule; Mongo userinfo with a raw `@` or space. The applog and setup writers cost about 2.3x develop on a normal 100 KB log (114 ms vs 50 ms, 46 ms vs 21 ms). **Command-line part built 2026-11-23 (not ticked):** `internal/secretscrub/flags.go` (run in `Mask` and `Carry` through `maskPend`): `--password x`, `--api-key x`, `/token x` (also quoted), sqlcmd/osql/bcp `-P x`, mysql `-pX`, redis-cli `-a x`, mongo tools `-p x`, curl `-u user:x`; a short option is judged only after its tool on the same line (`-p` is a port for psql). Tests in `flags_test.go` (table, ordinary commands left alone, paths and JSON left alone, hostile timing, random input). Review round 1 (2 must-fix, 6 should-fix) fixed: one-dash long options (`-Password x`), over-budget now fails closed, attached values (`-Phunter2`), sqlcmd `/P x`, line continuations, tool names after a backtick or `$(`, JSON and YAML argument lists, request paths (`POST /token`) and `;` or `|` ending a command. Review round 2 (4 must-fix, 7 should-fix) fixed: JSON argument arrays (also pretty printed), a command line inside a JSON string, PowerShell secure strings, values that ate the closing quote or tag, keytool/openssl/cmdkey pass options, sshpass/psexec/ldap/schtasks, curl clusters and `--user=`, tools after `|` `&&` `;` without spaces, XML arguments, help text and PowerShell flags no longer masked. Review round 3 fixed: JSON-escaped quotes (`\"`) in command lines inside JSON (Sysmon style), the colon and `=` forms no longer break JSON, `docker`/`podman`/`helm`/`az login -p`, closing brackets kept, secure strings in arrays; a test now checks that a command line inside valid JSON stays valid JSON. Still not covered: a password passed as a bare argument (`net use \\srv\share x`, `net user bob x`), smbclient `-U u%p`, `curl --oauth2-bearer`, and other tools' short options (`net use \\srv\share x`). Not tried on a real case export or log.

Kept readable: `token_count`, `max_tokens`, `password_min_length`, `tokenizer`, `TESTS_PASS: 120 of 120`, a connection string with no secret, text after an env-style value (`DB_PASSWORD=x DB_HOST=db`). Tests (all fail on the old code): table cases for each form and for ordinary text in every package, 1 MiB hostile inputs with a time bound, a 20,000-case random-forms test that plants a fake secret, a random no-panic test and a Go fuzz target (`FuzzMask`); BL-036 masking tests and the PR #167 tables are unchanged and green. **Unit-verified on Linux only; not run on a real export, log or release.** Left open on purpose: the older per-package patterns still run after the shared pass (kept so their exact output is unchanged); the PowerShell twin that writes the release log (`tools/release/ReleaseLib.ps1` `Protect-LogLine`) was not changed (no pwsh here), so a release log on disk can still hold the new forms until the page hides them on read; the Releases reader works line by line and carries only YAML block scalars and a value on the next line (`secretscrub.Carry`); a quoted value or JSON array that runs over several lines, and a tag split over lines, are not covered there (follow-up: SWP-1122-12); `addressOnly` (connection address shown after a reopen) was not touched. - [ ] **SWP-1122-13** [Medium, security] (PR #181 review 6, item 2) Secret scrubbers (`internal/secretscrub`, so every caller): a secret whose name and value are two fields of one record still leaks: the Kubernetes and Helm `env:` list (`- name: DB_PASSWORD` then `  value: x`), JSON `{"name":"DB_PASSWORD","value":"x"}` and the plist `<key>Password</key><string>x</string>`. Needs a record-aware reader (the sibling line, JSON member or element, with its own work budget and Carry state), so it is not built in #181. Listed under "Not covered" in `internal/secretscrub/doc.go`. **Built 2026-11-23 (not ticked):** `internal/secretscrub/record.go`, run first in `Mask` so every caller gets it: JSON `{"name":..,"value":..}` in either order, with other members, in arrays, pretty printed, the Python single-quote form, an object that never closes; the YAML list item `- name: DB_PASSWORD` with `value:` before or after it (inline, quoted, block scalar, next line, CRLF); the plist `<key>Password</key>` then `<string>`, `<data>`, `<integer>`, `<real>`. Each reader charges the work budget and fails closed; hostile inputs of 1 MiB run in 0.3 s in total; a random-input test never panics. The fresh security review (ten findings: a stray quote turning the JSON reader off, JSON inside a JSON string, uncharged scans, a memory table of lines, open records at budget end, multi-line Python repr, array values, values over 4 KiB, prose names, a quoted YAML scalar over two lines) is fixed and each has a test. Round 2 (2 must-fix, 8 should-fix): an unclosed array search is now charged, a full object stack drops its oldest object (cut log lines no longer switch the reader off), a quoted string has no 64 KiB cap, escaped mode closes only on a backslash run of 1 mod 4, a YAML value line is masked once, comments do not use the line window, a fail-closed edit starts where the first reader stopped, and `sortEdits` joins overlapping spans (shared with the XML reader). Round 3 (2 must-fix, 4 should-fix): JSON inside an apostrophe string is read, an apostrophe that never closes is not a string, a record keeps every "value" span it saw (a cut record or a duplicate key), a lone quote earlier on the line no longer mis-pairs the record, arrays and bare values have no 64 KiB cap and the array search skips strings. Not covered: a plist comment between key and value, `<key>API Key</key>` (white space in a plist name). Tests: `internal/secretscrub/record_test.go`, plus one through the real case-report scrubber (`TestScrubSecretsNameAndValueRecords`). Linux failures compared with the baseline: 0 new. Not covered, and listed in `doc.go` (follow-up SWP-1122-14): double-escaped JSON, Go `%+v`/`map[..]` prints, Python `EnvVar(name=.., value=..)`, HCL `env { name = .. }`, flow-style YAML `{name: DB_PASSWORD, value: x}`, a name and a value in different objects, a value under another member name, and the one-line-at-a-time Carry reader (the Releases log). Not tried on a real Helm chart, plist or app-settings export from a customer.

Each needs a reader with a work budget and a fail-closed end, like `record.go`. Found by the round-2 review of SWP-1122-13; no live sample of any of them yet. **Built 2026-11-23 (not ticked):** `internal/secretscrub/pairs.go` reads flow YAML `{name: X, value: y}`, Go `map[name:X value:y]` and `{Name:X Value:y}`, Python `EnvVar(name='X', value='y')` and HCL `env { name = "X" value = "y" }` (either order, over lines, inside a JSON string, a record that never closes), with the shared work budget; tests in `pairs_test.go` (leaks, readable settings stay, JSON stays valid, hostile timing) and four fuzz canaries. Minor, not a regression; add a tie-break on the original sample order and a test. **Built 2026-11-23 (not ticked until a real case is checked):** `M4Edges.AddSeq(t, seq, v)` orders values of one instant by `seq` (the case series passes the event's source line; equal sequence falls back to arrival order, as before); `TestM4SameInstantFollowsTheSourceLineNotTheDeliveryOrder` fails without the change (delivery 3,2,1 gave first=30 last=10) and passes with it; a test on the real SQLite store (`TestSeriesM4SameMillisecondFollowsTheSourceLine`, 12 rows in one millisecond, which Each returns in evidence_id order) fails without it (first=60 last=50) and passes with it; Blackbox points are fed in sample order and keep `Add`. - [ ] **BL-539** (found in the CI runs of PR #200) Flaky layout specs of the nightly e2e job (`e2e (ubuntu-24.04)`), none touched by that PR. One cause for the layout ones: under reduced motion (the setting every e2e spec runs with, and a real user's) `app.css` gives every element `transition-duration: .01 ms`, so a property that changes still starts a transition, and for the first two frames (about 30 ms) a read sees the box where it came from. (a) DONE in PR #200, UIFIX-18e read the error border in the same frame as the blur (run 45116106560: 2.98 against 3); it now polls the border and background together. (c) ROOT CAUSE FOUND, fixed in the spec, proven: SWEEP-F1-02 (run 45118414366, the label 8.84 px higher before the help opened). Opening More filters moves the `details` box onto its own row (`.scope-main .more-filters[open]`, `order` 0 to 5, `flex-basis` 100%), and for its first two frames the Run analysis row is still below the panel instead of on the first row beside the range controls, so the first row is 8.84 px shorter and the whole panel sits 8.84 px higher (details at 123 px, then 131.84 px; read 2 frames after the click: 123, 300 ms later: 131.84). Nothing grew above the panel; the spec read inside that window (3 of 15, 1 of 25 and 4 of 25 runs of a small script in this build, 0 of 40 in the spec itself, which is timed less tightly). PR #200 measured against the field grid, which moved with the panel; the spec now waits with the new `layoutSettled(page)` (`tools/e2e/support/ui.ts`: web fonts ready, at least 4 frames, then no finite animation or transition running for two frames, failing with the animations named after 5 s) and measures from the page again. What fixes the flake is those frames passing: the helper does not see layout moved by script or by data that arrives late, so if the 8.84 px comes back, suspect that first. Other layout specs that read a position right after a click or a resize may reuse it. 40 of 40 and 20 of 20 under 6 busy loops on 4 cores. No app change: the move is one frame pair under reduced motion only. (b) NOT REPRODUCED, cause unknown: UIFIX-18e "320 px: the page scrolls sideways" failed once (run 45101183787, before the wait for the resize to settle was added on that branch, PR #189) and has not failed since in the 4 later runs. In this build no frame after a resize to 1440, 1100, 390, 360 or 320 overflowed (every frame for 1.5 s, at 1, 6 and 20 times slower CPU, after the same theme and invalid-box steps), so it was never seen here and nothing is claimed about its cause. The wait before that read (2 frames, its timeout swallowed by `.catch(() => undefined)`) is replaced by `layoutSettled`, which fails loudly when the page keeps animating. The error-border read of the same spec is now one plain read after `layoutSettled` instead of a poll that accepted the first value of 3:1 or more. (d) SWEEP-F1-06 read the range chips before the app had drawn them once in 13 whole-file runs under 6 busy loops (`getComputedStyle` of null); it now waits for the chips first (40 of 40 under the same load). Tick this item when the nightly job has run clean for a week or (b) is caught with its list of wide elements (the failure message now names them). (source: PR #200 CI, 2026-11-23; BL-539 follow-up PR)

- [ ] **BL-542** Blackbox shows "Unreachable" after 4 or more quick "Check again" clicks while it runs (reported by the owner's developer 2026-11-23). Cause: each check built a new HTTP connection pool (the key keeper one per request, the direct reader one per check) and left its connection open; the recorder allows 8 open connections per address, so the next check was refused. Fix: one shared transport (max 4 connections and 2 idle per host, 10 s idle) in `blackbox.NewSharedTransport`, used by `NewHTTPSource` and the keeper `httpClient`; the recorder limit stays. Tests: `internal/blackbox/reader_conn_test.go`, `internal/keeper/bb_client_test.go`. Tick after the owner presses Check again 10 times on the real PC and the Blackbox stays Reachable. Note: the PC's installed Unravis must be updated to get the fix (client side only; the recorder needs no update).

## Daily security and performance sweep 2026-11-23 (read-only code review; nothing built, nothing ticked)

Scope: master changes of the last 7 days (about 260 commits) plus develop (54 commits ahead). Weighted to the update chain, the elevated Blackbox helper, the SQL setup flow, local HTTP surfaces and the always-on timers. Each item was checked by reading the code; none was run (Windows-only paths cannot run on Linux). Ids use `SWP-` so parallel branches do not collide on `BL-` numbers.

- [ ] **SWP-1123-1** [Low-Medium, security, develop only, not yet released] The typed SQL login is written to a folder that is not made private on Windows. `WriteSQLLoginFile("", ...)` (`internal/blackbox/sqllogin_file.go:33-55`, called from `internal/server/blackbox_sqlsetup.go`) uses `os.MkdirTemp` and `os.WriteFile` with 0700 and 0600. On Windows Go ignores those bits, so the plaintext password sits in `%TEMP%\unravis-sqllogin-*\login.json` with whatever ACL `%TEMP%` gives. The default `%TEMP%` is per user and safe. If `TEMP` or `TMP` points to a shared folder (for example `C:\Temp` or `C:\Windows\Temp`, common on servers), another local account can read the password while the administrator prompt is open (up to the 2-minute elevator timeout). The helper side is sound (it only reads, and binds the bytes to a SHA-256: `cmd/unravis-blackbox/sqllogin.go:73-110`), and the answer file of the same call is already made private through held handles (`openAnswerSlot`, `internal/blackbox/elevate_windows.go`). Fix: make the login folder the same way (create with a protected owner, SYSTEM and Administrators ACL through a handle, open the file without delete share until the call ends), or refuse a shared `TEMP` and use a per-user folder under `%LOCALAPPDATA%\Unravis`. Also open the wipe in `RemoveSQLLoginFile` by handle, not by path. Test on Windows with `TEMP` set to a folder another user can read.
- [ ] **SWP-1123-2** [Low, security hardening] Folder junctions (mount points) are not treated as links. Since Go 1.23 `os.Lstat` and `DirEntry.Type` report a junction as `ModeIrregular`, not `ModeSymlink` (`os/types_windows.go`, checked in the Go 1.24 source). These places test only `ModeSymlink`: `internal/server/open_folder.go:145,151` (doc comment says "must not be a reparse point"), `internal/server/blackbox_folders.go:102,116` (doc says "never follows a link"), `internal/blackbox/logs.go:44`. Failure: a junction named like a Blackbox folder on a fixed drive is listed and opened; Explorer opens the junction target. Impact is small (read-only listing and opening a folder the person can already open), so it is hardening. Fix: one helper `isLink(mode)` that tests `ModeSymlink|ModeIrregular` and use it in all three places; test with a junction made by `mklink /J` on Windows.
- [ ] **SWP-1123-3** [Low, performance, Releases app only] Every `/api/log` page request runs `foldRev` over the whole cached log (`internal/releasedash/api.go`, `steplog.go:357`): one regular expression per line, up to 400,000 lines for a CI log, for each 5,000-line page, and again on every 2-second live poll. The log itself is cached (`logCache`, up to 600,000 lines held in memory), the revision number is not. Fix: compute `foldRev` once when the cache entry is made (`readLogResult`) and read it from there. The app runs only while its window is open, so this is not always-on cost.

Known and not repeated: the Releases app session code is reusable and sits on the Edge command line (SWP-1122-7, accepted).

Checked and found sound (no item): signed feed fetch, mirror rules and rollback pins (`internal/update/fetch.go`); setup download (size, hash on write and from disk, setup file name rule) and the hand-off with the file held open (`download.go`, `launch_windows.go`, `updater_install.go`, `updater_auto.go`); Blackbox self-update (zip holds one program, size cap, hash against the signed program hash, `StartVerified`); elevated helper guard and answer file handling (`elevate_guard*.go`, `elevate_windows.go`); recorder status server (key first, throttle table bounded, connection limits, Origin and Sec-Fetch-Site refusal); the SQL access check (fixed statements, no driver text in answers); the pipe handler role rules (`internal/pipesrv`); locator file rules; machine journal read (`internal/setup/recover.go`); all `io.ReadAll` on request bodies have a size cap; CI workflows pin every action by SHA and pass inputs through `env`; no `{@html}` or `innerHTML` in `web/src`; recorder maps and the SQL folder are bounded (the host recordings by `Housekeep`, the SQL folder by `rotate` with the byte cap from `sqlDiskBounds`).

## Daily security and performance sweep 2026-11-25 (read-only code review; nothing built, nothing ticked)

Scope: code merged 2026-11-23 evening to 2026-11-24: the MCP grants, dispatcher, budgets and ledger (`internal/mcpsrv`, `internal/mcpaudit`, `internal/mcp`), the ag/ui pipe server and peer policy (`internal/pipesrv`, `internal/pipesec`), the CLI owner routes (`internal/clisrv`), the SQL ERRORLOG sampler and login file (`internal/blackbox/sqllog*.go`, `sqllogin_file.go`), the stored SQL login (`internal/bbsql/loginstore.go`), the keeper login hand-off, `internal/sealedfile`, the workflows, and the web polling. Each item was traced by reading the code; none was run (Windows paths are not run on Linux). Ranked most severe first. Ids use `SWP-1125-` so parallel branches do not collide on `BL-` numbers; renumber when merging.

- [ ] **SWP-1125-1** [Medium, security (audit integrity) + performance] One approved MCP host can flood the call ledger and push out the older rows, with two synchronous disk writes per call. `Dispatcher.CallAs` (`internal/mcpsrv/dispatch.go:208-261`) writes a ledger row for every call: a `denied` row for a tool outside the grant (line 236) and an `admit` row plus an `end` row for every allowed one. The only rate limit, `limiter.admit` (`internal/mcpsrv/budget.go:76`, called at dispatch.go:249), runs AFTER the tool check, and it counts only the live classes (12 comparisons and 120 other live calls an hour). Calls of the classes guide, list and cached, and every call for a tool outside the grant, have no count limit at all, and the pipe has no limiter either (`internal/pipesrv/pipesrv.go`). Each row is a separate statement under `PRAGMA synchronous=FULL` (`internal/mcpaudit/db.go:150`, `events.go:68`). Failure scenario: a looping or hostile host with a valid grant calls `audit_get_guide` (or a tool it may not use) in a tight loop; at a few hundred calls a second it writes the 100,000-row cap (`MaxEvents`, events.go:33) in minutes, `Prune` (every 1000 rows) deletes the oldest rows first, so the person's screen "Unravis > AI access > ledger" no longer shows what that host asked earlier, including its own earlier evidence calls; meanwhile the always-on app does two fsyncs per call. Proposed fix: (a) a per-profile call-rate limit for ALL classes, checked before any ledger write (for example 60 a minute, burst 20; a refusal says "do not loop"); (b) write at most one `denied` row per profile, tool and minute and keep a repeat count on it; (c) keep the cap per profile (for example the newest 20,000 rows each) so one host cannot evict another's rows; (d) test: 10,000 denied calls leave the older rows of a second profile untouched. (Related: BL-350 budgets, BL-351 prune.)
- [ ] **SWP-1125-2** [Low-Medium, performance and footprint] A finished MCP comparison stays in memory long after its ten-minute life. `Bridge.result` (`internal/mcp/tools.go:181`) holds the whole `*audit.Analysis` of the latest `audit_compare` of a profile. The TTL (`resultTTL`, bridge.go:23) is only checked when the next call reads it (`cached`, tools.go:95); nothing clears it on a timer. `LocalBackend` (`internal/mcpsrv/backend.go:30-62`) keeps one bridge per profile (up to `MaxProfiles` = 64) until the grant changes. Failure scenario: a host runs one comparison over a large source, goes quiet, and the tray app keeps that analysis (all groups, timelines) in RAM for days; with several hosts it multiplies. Proposed fix: drop `result` after the TTL with a `time.AfterFunc` set at line 183 (cancel and reset it on a new comparison), or a one-minute sweep in `LocalBackend` that calls a new `Bridge.expire()`; a test with the fake clock checks the field is nil after 10 minutes with no call.
- [ ] **SWP-1125-3** [Low-Medium, availability] The MCP enroll route can fill the host table with junk that never expires, and has no rate limit. `POST /mcp/v1/enroll` (`internal/mcpsrv/handler.go:27`, `Grants.Enroll` grants.go:182) creates a `pending` row for any new profile id with no credential; it is gated only by the pipe peer check (a process whose image is `unravis.exe` in the install folder, same user and session, which includes the CLI image). `insertPending` (grants.go:161) refuses past `MaxProfiles` = 64, and a pending row has no expiry (only the person's Remove clears it, grants.go:382). Failure scenario: a same-user script runs `unravis --mcp --mcp-profile <random>` 64 times; every real host then gets "Too many hosts are registered in Unravis" (429) until the person finds and removes 64 pending rows by hand; each enroll also costs a synchronous disk write. Proposed fix: expire a pending row nobody approved after 24 hours (delete at the next enroll and at open); cap pending rows separately (for example 16) so they cannot use the whole 64; a small per-minute limit on enroll and claim in `EnrollRoutes`; the approval card already shows "reported by the host", so add the pending age.
- [ ] **SWP-1125-4** [Low, security (denial of service on a shared PC)] Any signed-in account can hold the lock of the shared data folder forever. `lockSDDL` (`internal/sealedfile/platform_windows.go:97`) gives Authenticated Users and Service accounts `SYNCHRONIZE | MUTEX_MODIFY_STATE` (0x100001) on the machine-wide mutex `Global\Unravis-<sha256 of the lower-cased path>`. Acquiring a mutex needs only `SYNCHRONIZE`, and the name is computable from the shared folder path. `Lock` gives up after 5 s (so cases, hosts and ledger writes fail with "unavailable") and `LockWait` (line 122) never gives up until its context ends. Failure scenario: on a PC with two signed-in people (the shared data folder case, P14), the second account takes the mutex of the cases folder and sits on it; the first person's imports hang and saves fail until that process ends. The folder ACL, not the mutex ACL, is meant to decide who may write there. Proposed fix: grant the mutex only to the accounts the folder ACL lets write (SYSTEM, Administrators and the Unravis users group), or lock with an exclusive file handle (`LockFileEx` on a `.lock` file inside the folder) so the folder's own ACL decides; test on a two-account Windows run (owed to the owner's PC).
- [ ] **SWP-1125-5** [Low, privacy promise] The MCP ledger says "kept 90 days" but the 90-day prune runs only when the app starts and after every 1000 new rows in that run. `openMCPEndpoint` (`cmd/audit-explorer/mcpmode.go:47`) calls `Prune` once; `Record` calls it when the process-wide counter `inserted` (`internal/mcpaudit/events.go:85`) reaches a multiple of 1000, and the counter starts at 0 on every start. The tray app is meant to run for weeks. Failure scenario: a person who uses one host lightly (a few calls a day) and keeps the app open for months: rows older than 90 days stay on disk until a restart. Proposed fix: prune from a daily timer in the owner (reuse an existing housekeeping tick if one exists) or in `Record` when the last prune is over 24 hours old; add a fake-clock test.

Checked and found sound (no item): the ERRORLOG reader keeps only digits and fixed words, opens a regular file by handle with the final path compared, reads at most 256 KiB per instance per minute, caps instances and events, and drops lines dated more than two hours ahead (sqllog.go, sqllog_errorlog.go, sqllog_open_windows.go); the registry path filter (drive letter only, name `ERRORLOG`, no stream or share); the SQL login file (private folder, hash bound, wiped, swept at keeper start, made inside the keeper's own service-account profile, so other accounts cannot read it); the stored login (sealed, size bounded, refused unless the folder exists, printed as hidden); the ag pipe (clean path required, `Origin` refused, per-image gates for the CLI and MCP routes, session token only to the desktop image); the MCP credential (keyed digest, constant-time compare, state answered before the secret, body bounded, strict JSON, claim nonce kept on the first enroll only, fixed error texts); web polling (hidden-tab pacing already in `visibility.ts`); workflow permissions (`contents: read`, no `pull_request_target`). Accepted by design, not listed: the key keeper serves every interactive account of the PC (shared install), and a CLI route has no token (BL-318 option B, same-user peer check only).

## Daily security and performance sweep 2026-11-25, evening run (read-only code review; nothing built, nothing ticked)

Scope: master at `239ec0f4` (about 490 commits and 2000 files in 7 days, too many to read in full, so the review sampled the code that runs always or holds trust): the signed update chain and the Blackbox self-update (`internal/update`, `internal/blackbox/selfupdate.go`), the Blackbox store, TCP table, ERRORLOG and extras samplers, the Releases app server and CI views (`internal/releasedash`, `cmd/unravis-releases`), the local CI runner (`internal/localci`) and the CLI owner routes. Each item was traced by reading the code; none was run (Windows paths are not run on the Linux sweep box).

- [ ] **SWP-1125-6** [Medium, performance (CPU, disk reads), grows with history] The Releases app rebuilds every CI row from scratch on each poll, and the work grows without limit. The page asks `/api/overview`, `/api/runs` and `/api/machines` every 4 seconds while visible (`cmd/unravis-releases/ui/data.js:44-47,102`). Each call makes a new view (`internal/releasedash/ci.go:173`), then `rows()` (ci.go:411-430, called from `Page` at 551 and the overview at 1058) reads and parses EVERY request file of the queued, running and done folders (`ListRequests`, `internal/localci/read.go`; `folderOf`, `internal/localci/queue.go:235-261`; done requests are kept 14 days) and EVERY line of `runs/index.jsonl` (`ReadIndex`, `internal/localci/store.go:178-196`; never pruned, see SWP-1125-7). Then `fromIndex` (ci.go:342-348) runs about ten fields of every entry through `v.rd`, which runs the whole redaction regex set on each string (ci.go:207-212), and only after all that does `Page` cut to the requested 200 rows. Failure scenario: after a few months of runs (several hundred a week, more with the shadow agent and 2 PCs) a single poll parses thousands of files and tens of thousands of regex runs, three times in 4 seconds, so the Releases window and its Go process burn a core and the disk while it is simply open. Proposed fix: cache the built rows keyed by the (size, mtime) of the index file and of the three queue folders, and re-read only the tail of the index; build and redact only the rows that are returned (redact at output, not at load); give the poll a cheap "changed since" answer.
- [ ] **SWP-1125-7** [Medium, disk footprint, PC that runs CI] Nothing removes old run folders, old work folders or the caches, although the plan says it does. `Agent.prune` (`internal/localci/agent.go:538-546`) deletes only finished queue requests (`Queue.Prune`, 14 days). It does not touch: `runs/<id>/` (logs, `run.json`, `summary.md`) and `runs/index.jsonl` (append only, `store.go:156-170`); `work/<runID>` worktrees; `homes/<runID>` profiles; the `uci-<hash>` folders under the user's TEMP (`isolate.go:107-124`); the Go, module and npm caches and `caches-untrusted` (`store.go:201-215`). `docs/ci/LOCAL-PIPELINE.md:149` says "the agent prunes work areas older than 24 h and caches over a size limit": no code does (search: nothing reads `WorkPath()`, `CachesPath()` or `HomesPath()` to delete, only `RunPR` and `RunRef` remove their own worktree on a normal end, `prrun.go:280-284`, `runref.go:148-152`). Failure scenario: the agent is killed or the PC loses power or restarts mid-run (the memory notes say the PC sleeps and reboots): that run's worktree (a full checkout plus whatever the steps built, the plan itself says 3 to 6 GB per active run) and its `uci-*` temp folder stay for ever, and a requeued run gets a new id, so the next run never cleans them. The module and npm caches only grow. Proposed fix: at agent start and once an hour, delete `work/*`, `homes/*` and `uci-*` folders that no running request owns and that are older than 24 hours; trim `caches*` over a size limit (`go clean -modcache` style, or oldest-first by access time); add a run-folder retention (for example 60 days or 2000 runs, compress older step logs) and rewrite `index.jsonl` to match, one line per kept run.
- [ ] **SWP-1125-8** [Low, correctness of a log viewer] The Releases log search breaks the HTML escapes it highlights in. `hl()` in `cmd/unravis-releases/ui/app.js:382` escapes the line and then replaces the escaped search text inside the ESCAPED line. A search for `amp`, `lt`, `gt`, `quot` or `39` on a line that holds `&`, `<`, `>`, `"` or `'` matches inside `&amp;`, `&lt;`, `&gt;`, `&quot;`, `&#39;` and wraps part of the entity in `<mark>`, so the page shows the raw entity text (`&amp;`) instead of the character, and the hit count (computed on the raw line, app.js:379) no longer matches what is marked. Not an injection (only a `<mark>` tag is added, the CSP forbids inline script). Proposed fix: split the raw line on the search text first, escape each piece, and put the `<mark>` between them.
- [ ] **SWP-1125-9** [Low, hardening] `openBrowser` in the Releases app starts `rundll32` by bare name on Windows (`cmd/unravis-releases/main.go:145`), while the other launchers use the System32 path (`system32File` in `cmd/audit-explorer/main.go:672`, `internal/server/open_folder_windows.go:21`). A program named `rundll32.exe` earlier on the user's PATH would be started with the session link (which carries the session code). Same user only, so the gain is small, but it is a one-line change to match the rest. Proposed fix: use the full path under `%SystemRoot%\System32`. Also, the Edge window gets the session code on its command line (`window_windows.go:35`, `--app=<link>`); only an administrator can read another user's command line, so this is noted, not a finding.

Checked and found sound (no item): the Blackbox self-update (zip name and size checks, the single program taken from the zip, written with `O_EXCL`, hash compared with the signed manifest value, then `StartVerified` re-hashes the held-open handle before it starts the program, `selfupdate.go:477-540`, `launch_windows.go:116-139`); the Releases app server (loopback host check, constant-time session code, cookie `SameSite=Strict`, GET only, strict CSP, run ids and job names validated and the log folder listed from disk, never joined from the request, `ci.go:861-940`); log redaction (a long base64 run is hidden wherever it sits, so a log cut mid-key does not leak); the local CI checkout (git config and hooks rewritten before every fetch, `core.hooksPath` null, no symlinks, runner files taken from the base for non-owner pull requests, `checkout.go:38-100`); size caps on every `io.ReadAll` of outside data in the files changed this week (the two unbounded ones in `internal/server/case_key.go:180` and `masterkey.go:504` are behind `MaxBytesReader`); the Blackbox manifest lists are capped (gaps 1000, events 200, runs 200) and the ERRORLOG, TCP table and SQL extras samplers are bounded in bytes, rows, instances and retries; GitHub and feed polling in the Releases app uses ETags and a 30 second minimum gap. The SQL login file in a shared TEMP was checked and left out: the keeper runs as its own service account and uses its own profile temp (see the 2026-11-25 note above).

- [ ] **BL-590** Pre-push gate `tools/ci/prepush.sh`, Windows-risk scan `tools/ci/windows-risk.mjs`, PR template, thread gate and reviewer checklist (`docs/ci/THREAD-GATE.md`). Tick when three real pull requests from threads show the gate evidence and none of them went red on a cause the gate covers.
- [ ] **BL-591** Homestretch runs the Windows `go-tests` shard (and `desktop-sidecar` Pipe security tests) for the pull request, before the merge to develop. This removes the main cause (Windows-only failures found after the merge). Local-CI thread. Tick when a pull request with a Windows-only failure is stopped before the merge. Status 26 Nov: the local fast-gate `go` job already runs the affected packages on this Windows PC against the Windows baseline, so the PR-time Windows check exists in shadow; it becomes authoritative at phase 1. Gaps left: affected packages only, and the `desktop-sidecar` Pipe tests are not in the fast gate.
- [ ] **BL-592** Homestretch clean-profile host: empty case folder, no app running, so a baseline compare on the PC does not report host state as new failures (dry run 26 Nov: 20 "new" failures from a one-file change). Local-CI thread. Status 26 Nov: runs already use an isolated profile (home and case folder per run). Main false-NEW cause found: the PC drive was full. Added a low-disk guard (agent claims nothing below 10 GB free, trims the cache harder; flag `--min-free-gb`). Still open: a desktop app running on the PC is not isolated.
- [ ] **BL-593** Flaky quarantine: suspects (`unravis-sandbox.TestEscapeAttempts/fs` is FIXED 26 Nov, see BL-598), `blackbox.TestClumsyWrongReadKeyIsRefusedEveryWay` (server never listened under load, 26 Nov), `TestMaskLongNamesAreLinear`, `TestLockWaitHonoursContext`, casestore `TestEventsTimeBudgetAndCancel`, leakcheck ticker, CLI broker tests, `TestBucketCountsReadOneSnapshot`. Added 26 Nov (quarantined to 2026-12-10, fail only in a full-suite run on a loaded PC): `blackbox.TestClumsyEveryRouteIsGuardedAndRepeatedWrongKeysAreTurnedAway`, `casestore/sqlite.TestBucketCountsTimeoutIsPartial` (inject a clock), `dotnetdiag.TestWindowsDialRejectsForeignOwner`. Make each deterministic (clock injection, condition waits), then remove it from the baseline. Needs the two failing run links per test first. Status 26 Nov: quarantine file `docs/ci/flaky-tests.txt` read by `compare-go-failures.mjs` (id and `until=` date required; expired lines stop quarantining). Still open: make each suspect deterministic, then tick.
- [ ] **BL-594** Race job on the pull request for the packages a change touches (it runs only after the merge today; 13 logs of data races). Needs a C compiler on the runner that does it; local-CI thread decides where.
- [ ] **BL-595** CI gate follow-ups from the #326 review (all non-blocking): `windows-risk.mjs` shell-out rule misses `exec.CommandContext(context.Background(), "sh", ...)` (allow nested parentheses, add a self-test), misses `exec.Command("echo")`/`grep`, and its timing rule flags `< 1500ms` against a "about 1 s" checklist; `THREAD-GATE.md` says a baseline line "names the backlog item" but `compare-go-failures.mjs` matches whole lines (use a `# BL-nnn` comment line above the entry); `prepush.sh` says run twice, doc says three times; "ready to push" prints even with warnings or missing tools; `go test -tags tauri_sidecar .` has no baseline compare; paste block needs `git fetch origin develop`; step 4 (`test-promote-dist.mjs`, about 100 s) prints nothing while it runs.
- [ ] **BL-596** `tools/maint/Clean-Scratch.ps1`: scheduled daily run of the scratch and Go-cache cleanup (`-RegisterTask`), after the owner-visible first real run reports sizes freed. Tick when the task exists and one scheduled run has freed or skipped correctly. Superseded by BL-597 (hourly task and session hooks, same cleanup plus worktrees and an owner filter); this item closes when BL-597 ticks.
- [ ] **BL-597** Permanent housekeeping (owner order 2026-11-26): `tools/maint/Housekeeping.ps1` runs from the `Stop` and `SessionEnd` hooks (`.claude/settings.json`, throttled 50 and 15 minutes, detached, always exit 0) and from the hourly user task `Unravis-Housekeeping` (`-Install`). It cleans only this repository's finished sessions, `unravis-probe*`/`unravis-dev-*` in Temp, merged clean idle worktrees (never forced) and the Go cache over 3 GB, with a lock, one capped log line per run and `Test-Housekeeping.ps1`. Tick when the task exists on the PC and one run freed or skipped correctly; the hooks fire only in checkouts and sessions that hold the file (after the next promotion to `master`).
- [ ] **BL-598** Sandbox test harness read race (root cause of the "worker stopped without done: file already closed" failure of `unravis-sandbox.TestEscapeAttempts/fs`, 26 Nov). `cmd/unravis-sandbox/harness_test.go` read the worker's stdout from `cmd.StdoutPipe()` in one goroutine while another called `cmd.Wait()`; Go closes a StdoutPipe read end when `Wait` sees the process exit, so a reader that was a moment late got `read |0: file already closed` (or lost the last frames) because the worker exits right after its done frame. Test-harness only: the worker, its sandbox and its wire protocol are unchanged. It could only cause false failures in tests that need a done frame; `wantViolation` and `TestOneRunThenExit` (frames_test.go) also ended their read loops on ANY read error; the exit-code check backs `wantViolation` up (a violation run must exit 2, a done run exits 0), so no false pass was found, but `TestOneRunThenExit` could have missed a lost second done frame. Fixed: the harness owns the stdout pipe (`os.Pipe`, parent drops its copy of the write end, reader reaches the real EOF), both end only on `io.EOF` and fail on any other read error (defence in depth, clearer message); `runScenario` answers waiting calls only on the real no-frame timeout (`errNoFrame`), so a genuine read error is reported as itself; the canary-leak check in `TestEscapeAttempts` now also runs on a `script_error` end. Reproduced the exact error in a stand-alone Go program on Windows (200 of 200 with a slightly late reader on the old pattern, 0 of 200 with an own pipe); the sandbox suite passes with `-count=3 -cpu 1,4`. Seen on Linux too: the `TestEngineTimeDeadline/regexp` failure on the #328 CI run (job 139855069468) was this same race (`worker stopped without done: read |0: file already closed`), so it was never a timing flake. Not reproduced under `-race` locally (no Linux host on the PC). Tick after the fix has run green on GitHub CI a few times. Follow-up: check the other `StdoutPipe` users in tests (`cmd/audit-explorer/shutdown_windows_test.go`, `stdio_windows_test.go`, `internal/mcp/sdk_process_test.go`, `internal/ownerlease/lease_windows_test.go`, `internal/dotnetdiag/dial_linux_test.go`) for the same Wait-before-read pattern.

## Whole-project review 2026-11-26 (read-only code review; nothing built, nothing ticked; details in docs/review/)

Findings and evidence: `docs/review/FINDINGS.md`. Work items (W-nn) with files, tests, risk and model: `docs/review/PLAN.md`. Line numbers are for develop at `0d4fdbec`. Already tracked and not repeated here: RV-PERF-1 (SWP-1125-7), RV-CI-1 (BL-461), RV-CI-3 (baseline line for `TestSilentNoUAC`), RV-SEC-4 (SWP-1125-9).

- [ ] **RV-BB-1** [High, logic] Blackbox drops the whole sample, counter groups included, when the host read is busy or over its fixed 2 s budget; only the first failure raises an event (`internal/blackbox/groups.go:470,489-501`, `recorder.go:354-360`). Plan W-04 (needs W-03).
- [ ] **RV-UI-1** [High, UI] A fetch failure that is not the page's own timeout is rethrown as is, so a closed, updated or crashed app shows "Failed to fetch" with no way forward (`web/src/lib/api.ts:109-122`, `appState.svelte.ts:1072`). Plan W-01, W-02. **W-01 built 2026-11-26 (branch `claude/w01-unreachable-error`), part 1 only:** `request()` and the export download now turn a network failure (TypeError) into one `ApiError` (code `unreachable`, status 0) with the sentence in `web/src/lib/copy/unreachableCopy.ts`; unit tests only, not checked against a real app that was closed or updated. The banner that says the app has stopped is W-02; not ticked.
- [ ] **RV-REL-1** [High, UI] Releases dashboard keeps an old snapshot when a refresh fails and shows the error only when there is no snapshot; the freshness chip follows the fast poll only (`cmd/unravis-releases/ui/data.js:58-63`, `app.js:442`). Plan W-05.
- [ ] **RV-SEC-1** [Medium, security] Keeper `sql.import` accepts records without the checks `Save` runs, so a local user can plant a saved connection whose label and host look right but whose stored server points elsewhere (`internal/sqlconn/sqlconn.go:432-465`, `internal/keeper/backend.go:75,210-219`). Reproduced with a throwaway test. Plan W-06.
- [ ] **RV-SUP-1** [Medium, logic] Update state "launched" never times out when setup dies without writing a result; the automatic path does nothing in that state (`internal/server/updater_auto.go:230-232`, `updater.go:255-260`). Plan W-07.
- [ ] **RV-SRV-2** [Medium, logic] Archive and verify jobs are neither cancelled nor waited for at shutdown (`internal/server/import_jobs.go:324-330`, `archive_jobs.go:193,232`, `cmd/audit-explorer/main.go:384`). Plan W-08. Code and tests done on branch claude/w08-jobs-stop-shutdown (shutdown stops and waits 5 s; tested on the real job path); not ticked until a live shutdown during an archive is seen.
- [ ] **RV-SRV-1** [Medium, logic] A start-up reconnect failure is cached for 2 minutes and replayed when the person presses Start tracing, without dialling (`internal/server/reconnect.go:30-31,449-452`). Plan W-09. **Built 2026-11-26 (branch `claude/w09-reconnect-dials`), unit-tested only (`TestAPersonsReconnectDialsEvenWhenTheStartupPassCachedAFailure`, `TestStartTracingDialsAgainAfterTheStartupPassFailed`); not checked on a live case with a database that fails at start-up and is then fixed.**
- [ ] **RV-BB-2** [Medium, logic, owner decision] The Blackbox free-space rule can delete every recording; no minimum is kept (`internal/blackbox/housekeeping.go:117-133`). By design A37; needs the owner's ruling (PLAN O-1).
- [ ] **RV-BB-7** [Medium, logic] Blackbox collect and write failures raise one event at the start and none after; nothing says "still failing" or "recovered" (`internal/blackbox/recorder.go:354-360,383-398`). Plan W-03. Built on branch claude/w03-blackbox-failing-recovered (still-failing event every 15 min, recovered event, plain wording in the charts panel); not ticked until seen on a real recorder run.
- [ ] **RV-BB-9** [Medium, logic] "Paused for low disk" is read from the newest 200 events, so it is lost after 200 later events while recording is still paused (`internal/blackbox/state.go:216-227`, `manifest.go:28`). Plan W-11.
- [ ] **RV-BB-10** [Medium, logic] The Blackbox service has no crash capture (`cmd/unravis-blackbox`). Related: BL-315. Plan W-12.
- [ ] **RV-DAT-1** [Medium, logic] One bad record makes the whole saved-connections file unreadable, delete included; "Try again" cannot help (`internal/history/history.go:334-376`, `web/src/lib/copy/sourcesCopy.ts:34`). Plan W-13.
- [ ] **RV-DAT-2** [Medium, performance] The interval join's backward scan degrades to intervals times events when one long event sits early in the data (`internal/join/join.go:62-103`). Benchmark first. Plan W-14.
- [ ] **RV-UI-2** [Medium, UI] The update bar ignores failed polls and keeps a stale state such as "being applied" (`web/src/lib/updateState.svelte.ts:25-33,125-130`). Plan W-02.
- [ ] **RV-UI-3** [Medium, UI] Export says "saved" when the download is only started, and re-enables Save on a preview the server has used up (`web/src/components/ExportPanel.svelte:59-86`). Plan W-15.
- [ ] **RV-UI-4** [Medium, UI] Removing a saved connection has no confirm; in the SQL Server form it also swallows errors (`web/src/components/wizard/SqlServerForm.svelte:101-104`). Plan W-16.
- [ ] **RV-UI-5** [Medium, UI] Open-by-code answers "not found" for every failure, network and lock included (`web/src/lib/caseCode.ts:51-67`). Plan W-17.
- [ ] **RV-UI-6** [Medium, UI] The help panel's update line calls every error "could not reach the update server" (`web/src/components/Glossary.svelte:82`). Plan W-17.
